# Ezekiel: a record of how this book's unit boundaries were decided, and what was disputed

This document records the division of the book of Ezekiel into units, the disagreements that arose while that division was reviewed, the evidence offered on each side of every disagreement, how each was settled, and what remains unsettled. It is written for a reader who was not present and who owes the process no trust: every claim about the Hebrew text below is quoted or mechanically derived from a review record listed in the provenance table, and the words that are the apparatus's own rather than a reviewer's are enumerated at the end.

Two witnesses are used throughout. The consonantal and pointed Hebrew is the Open Scriptures Hebrew Bible edition of the Westminster Leningrad Codex; the English is the World English Bible. Neither is emended here. Where the two number verses differently — and in Ezekiel they do — both numbers are given.

## 1. What made this book difficult

**The two witnesses do not number this book alike.** The Hebrew and the English diverge across chapters 20 and 21: Hebrew 21:1-5 corresponds to English 20:45-49, and Hebrew 21:6-37 to English 21:1-32. Every boundary in that stretch therefore has two addresses, and an argument that names only one of them cannot be checked by a reader holding the other witness. This record gives both.

**The book is long and its repertoire of boundary signals is narrow.** The text runs to 1273 verses in 48 chapters. Its unit boundaries are carried by a small set of recurring formulae rather than by varied literary signals, so the same few devices must do almost all the work, and a boundary argument usually turns on which instance of a repeated formula is doing which job.

Counted in the witness, the recurring devices include the word-event formula (48); dated oracles (14); year-words that are not datelines (11); calendar dates that are not datelines (4).

**The measuring vision resists ordinary division.** The closing chapters record a guided tour with repeated motion verbs and repeated measurements, in which the natural breaks are architectural rather than rhetorical. Several disagreements below concern that stretch, and they are disagreements about whether a change of place is a change of unit.

**The confidence recorded on each unit is part of the evidence, not a summary of it.** Across the 145 units, the recorded confidence is medium 56, medium_low 52, high 22, low 15. A unit marked low is not a unit carelessly divided; it is one where the witness itself offers weak or one-sided evidence for the division, and the record says so rather than presenting a confident face.

## 2. How the division was reviewed, and what that does and does not establish

The division was reviewed by two independent readings of every unit, neither able to see the other's findings: one working from the Hebrew witness and its accentuation and section marks, one working from the English and the structural apparatus. Their findings were then re-read by a third set of readings covering every unit, and a fourth reading audited those. Each stage was recorded before the next began, and the text under review was fixed by digest throughout, so no stage could quietly revise the object the earlier stage had examined.

The review covered every unit: 44 independent readings across 22 groups, and 11 further readings covering all 145 units.

**What this does not establish.** The two initial readings were independent of each other, and that is the strength of the arrangement. The later readings were not independent in the same way: they read the earlier findings. Agreement between them is therefore not evidence that a finding is correct, only that it was not contradicted, and this record never treats such agreement as corroboration. One stage of the review also received descriptions of what earlier readers had found; the adjudicating record held that this biases what a reader looks at rather than what the text says, and declined to discard the round on that ground while recording that its internal agreement cannot be read as independence. That limitation is repeated here because it affects how much weight the reader should give to unanimity below.

Of the 145 units, 131 carry a recorded disagreement.

## 3. The disputes, the evidence on each side, and the resolution

Each entry below names the passage, states the question as a reviewer raised it, gives the evidence offered on each side in that reviewer's own words, and then gives the resolution from whichever record settled it. Where nothing settled it, the entry says so and the matter reappears in section 5.

### Ezekiel 4:1-17

*Recorded confidence: medium_low. Severity of the disagreement as the reviewer graded it: high.*

**Reading from the English and the structural apparatus** — challenge, severity medium.

> The span Ezek.4.1-Ezek.4.17 is a coherent commanded-action complex and it holds, but the row rejects the 4:1-3 against 4:4-17 division without naming either piece of evidence that stands on that division's close side: a samekh is recorded after MT 4:3, and Ezek.4.3 ends on the sign colophon א֥וֹת הִ֖יא לְבֵ֥ית יִשְׂרָאֵֽל ('it is a sign to the house of Israel'), the free-standing noun for 'sign' standing on 1 verse in the book (sweep: the free-standing token, 1 verse, Ezek.4.3). A samekh after MT 4:15 is likewise inside the span and unnamed, while the samekh marks after MT 4:12 and MT 4:14 are disclosed. The row also says the continuing commands open with a bare 'and you' at 4:4 and 4:9; Ezek.4.3 opens the same way (וְאַתָּ֤ה קַח לְךָ֙), so the span carries three such commands, not two.

Evidence on each side, as this reader set it out:

> Seam 4:3/4:4 weighed from both sides. Close side: Ezek.4.3 ends on the sign colophon and a samekh stands after MT 4:3, which together make it the one interior point in the span with a genre label and a Masoretic break behind it. Onset side: Ezek.4.4 opens וְאַתָּ֤ה שְׁכַב֙, a bare 'and you' with an imperative beginning a different symbolic action, the bearing of the iniquity of the house of Israel by lying on the side; that construction is not among the devices the book's ruled granularity licenses for a row onset, so the mark has no formula opposite it and does not cut. The WEB paragraph standing at web:Ezek.4.4 is translation-layer texture and carries no boundary weight. The span therefore holds at 17 verses with its cap disclosure, and the close-side evidence belongs on the record because it is the whole of the case for the alternative the row names and rejects. refs_mirror flag on this row: the unmirrored refs are Ezek.3.25, Ezek.3.27 and Ezek.5.1, the adjacent units' anchor verses argued in prose; no literary-form defect in them.

What this reader proposed: {'fact': 'a samekh stands after MT 4:3 and Ezek.4.3 closes on the sign colophon, both belonging to the weighing of the 4:1-3 alternative; a samekh also stands after MT 4:15', 'verse': 'Ezek.4.3'}

**Reading from the Hebrew witness** — challenge, severity high.

> The single witness records a closed-section mark on MT 4:3 — the verse that ends the tile act with the sign declaration א֥וֹת הִ֖יא לְבֵ֥ית יִשְׂרָאֵֽל — and a second one on MT 4:15; the row names only MT 4:12 and MT 4:14 among the closed-section marks inside 4:1-4:17, and it refuses the 4:3/4:4 division from the onset side alone. The mark on MT 4:3 falls exactly on the division the row rejects.

Evidence on each side, as this reader set it out:

> Behind the 4:3/4:4 seam: MT 4:3 ends on the sign declaration to the house of Israel and the witness marks a closed section there. Ahead of it: MT 4:4 opens וְאַתָּ֤ה שְׁכַב֙, the bare pronoun with a fresh lying command, without the 'and you, son of man' address that stands at 4:1 and at 5:1 — but MT 4:3 itself opens וְאַתָּ֤ה קַח לְךָ֙, the same bare pronoun with the same 'take for yourself' imperative, so the onset test the row applies does not separate 4:3 from 4:4 either. Carrying the span forward: the ropes at 4:8 and one rationing arc to 4:17, whose open-section mark the row does cite. A closed-section mark informs and never decides, so the span can stand; it cannot stand on the stated ground, which weighs only what 4:4 lacks.

What this reader proposed: {'fact': 'a closed-section mark on MT 4:3, whose final clause is the sign declaration, and a second closed-section mark on MT 4:15', 'verse': 'Ezek.4.3, with Ezek.4.15'}

**Resolution.**

- On the second reading of this passage: the reading from the Hebrew witness's severity (high) is the better-grounded reading and the reading from the English and structural apparatus's (medium) understates the class. The two lanes agree on the facts; they differ on what rule those facts engage, and the rule is the duty to weigh a competing division, not the requirement to disclose a section mark in each direction it bears on alone. The span stands; I propose the confidence be reconsidered.
- From the witness: MEASURED: SAMEKH on 4:3, SAMEKH on 4:12, SAMEKH on 4:14, SAMEKH on 4:15, PE on 4:17. The row discloses 4:12, 4:14 and 4:17 and NOT 4:3 or 4:15. Under a standing review requirement the samekh on 4:3 stands at the 4:3/4:4 seam, which is EXACTLY the cut this row's strongest_rejected_alternative field rejects ('Splitting the tile sign-act (4:1-4:3) from the lying and food-rationing sign-acts (4:4-4:17)'). the duty to weigh a competing division is in terms: a mark standing exactly at a cut the row rejects 'must be named and weighed inside the rejected-alternative field'. It is not named there or anywhere. That makes this an the duty to weigh a competing division breach, not merely an undisclosed interior mark, and the duty to weigh a competing division's own words put the duty ABOVE disclosure - which is why OL's higher severity is right. The samekh on 4:15 is a second, plainer the requirement to disclose a section mark in each direction it bears on interior omission. The row's discriminating test also fails on the bytes: it holds the three stages together because '4:4 and 4:9 continue with the bare imperative ve'attah rather than the son of man sub-onset construction that opens 4:1', but MEASURED oshb:Ezek.4.3 itself opens וְאַתָּ֤ה קַח לְךָ֙ - the same bare ve'attah with the same take-for-yourself imperative as 4:9 (וְאַתָּ֣ה קַח לְךָ֡), while 4:4 opens וְאַתָּ֤ה שְׁכַב֙. So the span carries three bare-ve'attah commands, not two, and the test does not separate 4:3 from 4:4. Both lanes found this and I confirm it. What DOES hold the span is untouched: MEASURED neither 4:4 nor 4:9 is a member of any pinned onset class, and 4:1, 4:16 and 5:1 are the son_of_man members in this stretch. TWO FURTHER MEASURED POINTS. (1) The row states the 4:8 rope recurrence as 'the exact word from 3:25' with NO tier, where Ezekiel 3:22-27 states the same recurrence at the accent_stripped tier; the byte tier is false, so this is a provenance overstatement of the the standing requirement that every claim carry its true evidential status kind, and the reading from the Hebrew witness is the only lane that named it. (2) a standing review requirement: the row measures a chapter-4 span against 'the 14-16 stretch cap for measured or listed blocks', but a standing review requirement confines that allowance to chs 40-42, 45 and 48 and makes the 7-10 target the benchmark elsewhere; the row never states the 7-10 benchmark. NEITHER LANE NAMED THIS, and the reading from the English and structural apparatus reproduces the same cap framing in its own prose ('at 17 verses the span is past the ruled stretch'). a standing review requirement/a standing review requirement satisfied (paseq 4:9; K/Q at 4:6 and 4:15 both disclosed; the untyped note at 4:6 disclosed). a standing review requirement: MEASURED four WEB-identical runs, the largest 8 words at 4:1 and at 4:16, none properly delimited.
- On audit: CONFIRM
- Adjudicated: DECLINED — medium_low stands (proposed: low (a reader of a later round))

### Ezekiel 5:1-17

*Recorded confidence: medium_low. Severity of the disagreement as the reviewer graded it: high.*

**Reading from the English and the structural apparatus** — challenge, severity medium.

> The span Ezek.5.1-Ezek.5.17 holds and closes correctly at 5:17, but Ezek.5.13 is weighed at the weaker of its two values on exactly the question the row is answering. Its close reads וְֽיָדְע֞וּ כִּי אֲנִ֣י יְהוָ֗ה דִּבַּ֨רְתִּי֙ ('and they shall know that I, Yahweh, have spoken'): the recognition refrain in its third-person form, which the device census lists among the 28 strict-form recognition verses, fused with the emphatic 'I Yahweh have spoken'. The row names 5:13 only as one of the three occurrences of the emphatic close, so the span's strongest interior close device is never identified as a refrain, in a row whose close argument turns on which of the three occurrences is unit-final.

Evidence on each side, as this reader set it out:

> Seam 5:13/5:14 weighed from both sides. Close side: Ezek.5.13 carries two refrain-grade devices at once, the recognition refrain and the emphatic close, which is stronger than any other interior point in the span. Onset side: Ezek.5.14 opens וְאֶתְּנֵךְ֙ לְחָרְבָּ֣ה ('and I will make you a waste'), a continuation with no messenger formula, word-event or address restarting the speech, so nothing licenses an onset there and the unit cannot end at 5:13. The close therefore stays at 5:17, where the emphatic formula stands with a pe recorded after MT 5:17. A second alternative the row does not weigh: the 5:4/5:5 division between the commanded action and its interpretation, with a pe recorded after MT 5:4 on the close side and the messenger formula opening Ezek.5.5 on the onset side. It does not cut, because no refrain-grade close stands at 5:4 and the interpretation is bound to the action by the clause shared between Ezek.5.2 and Ezek.5.12 (sweep: 3 verses carry that clause, the third at Ezek.12.14); but at 17 verses the span is past the ruled stretch, and that is the alternative the weighing should address. refs_mirror flag on this row: the unmirrored refs are the whole of Ezek.4.1-Ezek.4.17 plus Ezek.6.1 and Ezek.12.14, produced by a verse range cited in prose and by two cross-unit anchors; no literary-form defect in them.

What this reader proposed: {'fact': "Ezek.5.13 carries the recognition refrain as well as the emphatic 'I Yahweh have spoken', and is paragraph-final rather than unit-final because Ezek.5.14 opens no new speech", 'verse': 'Ezek.5.13'}

**Reading from the Hebrew witness** — challenge, severity high.

> The row's stated ground — that none of the section marks inside 5:1-5:17 coincides with a formula onset — is false on the witness at four seams: the open-section mark on MT 5:4 stands before the messenger formula the row itself cites at 5:5 (כֹּ֤ה אָמַר֙ אֲדֹנָ֣י יְהֹוִ֔ה); the closed-section marks on MT 5:6 and MT 5:7 stand before the two 'therefore' messenger formulas at 5:7 (לָכֵ֞ן כֹּֽה אָמַ֣ר אֲדֹנָ֣י יְהוִ֗ה, addressing a plural) and at 5:8 (לָכֵ֗ן כֹּ֤ה אָמַר֙ אֲדֹנָ֣י יְהוִ֔ה הִנְנִ֥י עָלַ֖יִךְ, addressing a feminine singular); and the open-section mark on MT 5:10 stands before the oath and the utterance signature at 5:11. Separately, MT 5:13 reads וְֽיָדְע֞וּ כִּי אֲנִ֣י יְהוָ֗ה דִּבַּ֨רְתִּי֙ — the third-person recognition refrain governing 'I Yahweh have spoken' — so the verse the row rejects as unit-final carries a stacked close, and its recognition member is nowhere disclosed.

Evidence on each side, as this reader set it out:

> At 5:4/5:5: behind, the burning and scattering of the hair ends and the witness marks an open section; ahead, the messenger formula opens the interpretation — so a mark and a formula onset do meet there. At 5:7/5:8: behind, a plural address; ahead, a feminine singular address after a closed-section mark, which is a person-and-number shift rather than an absence of coincidence. At 5:13/5:14: behind, a stacked close, recognition refrain plus 'I Yahweh have spoken'; ahead, MT 5:14 opens וְאֶתְּנֵךְ֙ לְחָרְבָּ֣ה with no onset device at all, and no section mark stands at 5:13. That pairing is what actually holds the span together, along with the clause closing both 5:2 and 5:12, וְחֶ֖רֶב אָרִ֥יק אַחֲרֵיהֶֽם, word-for-word and verse-final in each. The span keeps its extent; the ground given for it does not survive.

What this reader proposed: {'fact': "the open-section mark on MT 5:4 falls at the seam where 5:5's messenger formula opens the interpretation, and MT 5:13 carries the third-person recognition refrain governing 'I Yahweh have spoken'", 'verse': 'Ezek.5.4 with Ezek.5.5; Ezek.5.13'}

**Resolution.**

- On the second reading of this passage: the reading from the Hebrew witness's challenge (high) stands and is the decisive lane; the reading from the English and structural apparatus (medium) missed the row's load-bearing false statement entirely and raised only part of it as an uncertainty. This is the clearest case in my two clusters where the two correlated lanes do NOT converge, and the witness settles it for OL. The span stands; I propose the confidence be reconsidered.
- From the witness: The row's device_notes say: 'Marks after 5:4 (pe), 5:6, 5:7, 5:9 (samekh) and 5:10 (pe) are disclosed as intra-row, single-witness texture; none coincides with a formula onset, so none is read as a unit-cutting seam.' MEASURED, mark by mark, applying a standing review requirement's direction rule: the PE on 5:4 stands at 5:4/5:5 and MT 5:5 is in the pinned messenger 122-list; the SAMEKH on 5:6 stands at 5:6/5:7 and MT 5:7 is in that list; the SAMEKH on 5:7 stands at 5:7/5:8 and MT 5:8 is in that list; the PE on 5:10 stands at 5:10/5:11 and MT 5:11 is the ONLY member of the pinned utterance 81-list in chs 1-8, which the row itself calls 'an emphatic sub-onset'. Only the SAMEKH on 5:9 does not meet a pinned onset - MT 5:10 is in no class. So FOUR of the five interior marks coincide with a formula onset and the row's blanket denial is false four times over. the reading from the Hebrew witness named exactly those four; the reading from the English and structural apparatus named none of them in its finding and raised only the 5:4/5:5 division in its uncertainty field. LF's own finding - that MT 5:13 carries a recognition member and not only the emphatic close - is also correct and I confirm it independently: MEASURED, Ezek.5.13 is in BOTH the pinned recognition 28-list AND the pinned i-YHWH-have-spoken 14-list, and the row cites it only as one of the three emphatic occurrences. The governing class for that one is the duty to weigh a competing division: the row's rejected alternative names 5:13 as a live candidate but names only one of the two devices that would carry it. WHAT HOLDS THE SPAN IS UNTOUCHED AND I CONFIRM IT: MEASURED, MT 5:14 is in no pinned onset class, and the clause closing 5:2 and 5:12 is BYTE-IDENTICAL (וְחֶ֖רֶב אָרִ֥יק אַחֲרֵיהֶֽם) with the sweep returning exactly three verses, 5:2, 5:12, 12:14 - so the row's recurrence claim is right at the byte tier. Mark duty under the requirement to disclose a section mark in each direction it bears on is COMPLETE in coverage (onset seam 4:17 disclosed, all five interior marks disclosed, close seam 5:17 disclosed) and defective in its weighing. a standing review requirement: the row again measures against 'the 14-16 stretch cap' where a standing review requirement confines that allowance to chs 40-42, 45 and 48; neither lane named it. a standing review requirement/a standing review requirement satisfied (paseq 5:1 and 5:7 disclosed; MEASURED no K/Q site in ch 5, and the row claims none). a standing review requirement: MEASURED an 8-word run at 5:1 and five-word runs at 5:7 and 5:8, none properly delimited.
- On audit: CONFIRM
- Adjudicated: DECLINED — medium_low stands (proposed: low (a reader of a later round))

### Ezekiel 10:9-17

*Recorded confidence: low. Severity of the disagreement as the reviewer graded it: high. The two initial readings reached different conclusions.*

**Reading from the English and the structural apparatus** — challenge, severity low.

> The stated ground for holding this row at low is not what the bytes carry. Ezek.10.9 opens on the same sight-formula that opens Ezek.10.1, and as an opener that formula stands at four verses book-wide (Ezek.2.9, 8.2, 10.1, 10.9). The onset therefore rests on the device class already accepted as the opener at Ezek.10.1, and the span Ezek.10.9-10.17 stands as drawn.

Evidence on each side, as this reader set it out:

> Onset side: the sight-formula at Ezek.10.9 introduces the four wheels in the same construction that introduces the throne at Ezek.10.1, and what follows is sustained description. Close side: Ezek.10.17 ends that description on the summary clause about the spirit of the living creature, and Ezek.10.18 restarts narrated action with the glory leaving the threshold. Neither side carries a parashah mark, since ch 10 carries none, so the seam rests on the sight-formula at the onset and the description-to-narration turn at the close, which is the same weight of evidence the unit at Ezek.10.1-10.8 is held at.

What this reader proposed: {'fact': 'the sight-formula opening Ezek.10.1 also opens Ezek.10.9; as an opener it stands at four verses (Ezek.2.9, 8.2, 10.1, 10.9)', 'verse': 'Ezek.10.9'}

**Reading from the Hebrew witness** — challenge, severity high.

> The exclusivity that certifies this unit is false on the bytes. The wheel vocabulary does recur outside MT 10:6-17 and MT 1:15-21: the wheel word stands at MT 3:13 (הָאוֹפַנִּ֖ים), MT 10:19 (וְהָאֽוֹפַנִּ֖ים) and MT 11:22 (וְהָאֽוֹפַנִּ֖ים), and the whirling-wheel word at MT 10:2 (לַגַּלְגַּ֜ל), MT 23:24 (וְגַלְגַּל֙) and MT 26:10 (וְגַלְגַּ֜ל). Two of those, MT 10:2 and MT 10:19, lie inside the units on either side of this one, which is the opposite of the lexical self-containment the unit rests on. The unit states in the same breath that no formula marks its onset, so this claim is its only affirmative ground.

Evidence on each side, as this reader set it out:

> Onset side: MT 10:9 opens with the same looking-and-behold clause as MT 10:1, carries no formula and no mark, and the preceding close at MT 10:8 has none either; the whole chapter sits inside one Masoretic section running from MT 10:1 to MT 11:1, so no mark bears on either seam. Close side: MT 10:17 ends the description with its summary clause and MT 10:18 resumes narrated action with the glory going out. Both seams therefore rest on the alternation between narrated action and sustained description alone — a real signal, and why the span can stand at its already-low level — but the lexical argument offered to reinforce them does not survive the bytes and must be withdrawn rather than reworded.

What this reader proposed: {'fact': 'the wheel vocabulary crosses both adjacent seams, the whirling-wheel word at MT 10:2 and the wheel word at MT 10:19, and it also stands at MT 3:13, MT 11:22, MT 23:24 and MT 26:10, so the ground for this unit is the alternation between narrated action and sustained description, not lexical exclusivity', 'verse': 'Ezek.10.19'}

**Resolution.**

- On the second reading of this passage: OL's challenge stands and is decisive; LF's fact is measured true but its proposed confidence move does not follow, because the device it rests on is not in the inventory. I propose: no confidence increase, the exclusivity sentence withdrawn rather than reworded, and LF's formula routed to the inventory as a candidate class.
- From the witness: MEASURED Ezek_oshb.txt. The wheel vocabulary is NOT confined to 10:6-17 and 1:15-21: אופן stands at 14 verses (1:15, 1:16, 1:19, 1:20, 1:21, 3:13, 10:6, 10:9, 10:10, 10:12, 10:13, 10:16, 10:19, 11:22) and גלגל at 5 (10:2, 10:6, 10:13, 23:24, 26:10). Outside the two zones the row names: 3:13, 10:19, 11:22, 10:2, 23:24, 26:10 - exactly OL's six. Worse for the row than OL says: 10:2 sits inside Ezekiel 10:1-8 and 10:19 inside Ezekiel 10:18-22, the rows on either side, and the row's own claimed zone '10:6-17' already reaches back into Ezekiel 10:1-8's span, so even on its own framing the 'self-contained descriptive block' shares vocabulary with its neighbour. Since the row states in the same breath that no formula marks its onset, this exclusivity was its only affirmative ground, so OL's HIGH severity is earned and its 'withdrawn rather than reworded' is right: rewording keeps a claim the bytes deny. LF's fact is equally measured: the verse-initial sight formula ואראה opens exactly 4 verses book-wide (2:9, 8:2, 10:1, 10:9), so 10:9 opens on the same construction as 10:1. But that formula is NOT a class in ezek_device_inventory.json at its pinned digest - the formulae keys are word_event (x3), son_of_man, recognition (x2), thus_says, utterance, hand_of_yhwh, set_your_face, i_am_yhwh_spoken, and no sight formula. The row's own sentence 'nothing in the book's formula inventory marks 10:9 as an onset' is therefore MEASURED TRUE as written. Under a standing review requirement a lane that finds a candidate member reports it against the inventory, not against the row, and under the brief's counts rule a sweep with no inventory list behind it is not a measured class. So LF's four-verse sweep is a candidate class for the inventory and not a ground to move this row's confidence; and with the row's only affirmative ground withdrawn, confidence certainly does not rise.
- On audit: CONFIRM
- Adjudicated: LF's raise REFUSED — low stands (proposed: LF: low→medium_low; a reader of a later round: no change)

### Ezekiel 11:14-21

*Recorded confidence: high. Severity of the disagreement as the reviewer graded it: high. The two initial readings reached different conclusions.*

**Reading from the English and the structural apparatus** — challenge, severity high.

> The category note denies that gather-and-return is stated in the span, and 11:17 states it in that device's own vocabulary — gather from the peoples, assemble out of the countries where they were scattered, give them the land of Israel — with 11:18 completing it by their coming there. The denial is contradicted by the verse it stands over, and it is offered as the ground of the unit type that the verse in fact establishes outright. Second, the two samekh marks the unit cites stand immediately in front of the two messenger-formula paragraph onsets at 11:16 and at 11:17, and neither formula is named; at 11:17 the person also turns from third-person speech about the exiles to second-person-plural address to them, so 11:17 is a candidate interior seam corroborated by both a mark and a formula, and it is never weighed — the rejected alternative weighs only the opposite direction, a longer row.

Evidence on each side, as this reader set it out:

> Front seam 11:13/11:14: the close side is Pelatiah's death and the prophet's cry with a pe against 11:13; the onset side is the strict word-event form at 11:14 with the addressee turning to the exiles. That side is strong. Back seam 11:21/11:22: the close side is the utterance signature at 11:21; the onset side carries no onset device of the book's vocabulary, because the cherubim-and-glory clause of 11:22 repeats clauses already narrated at 10:19 and is a resumption rather than an opening. Interior candidate 11:16/11:17: the onset side has the messenger formula, the samekh in front of it, and the turn to second-person-plural address; the close side ends on the sanctuary-for-a-little-while clause with no close-class device, so that seam is evidenced from one side only and the cut is declined. Two seams evidenced from one side each, an unweighed interior candidate, and an enclosing vision that has not yet returned the prophet do not carry a top-level confidence; the formula bracket is genuine, which is why the level lands at medium and not lower. The out-of-span verses this unit argues from are the neighbouring scene and the vision's return, which the two-sided test requires it to weigh.

What this reader proposed: {'fact': '11:17 states the gather-and-return device directly, so the salvation classification rests on the verse itself and needs no analogy; the messenger formula stands at 11:16 and again at 11:17, each with a samekh entered against the verse in front of it, and the 11:17 paragraph turns to second-person-plural address.', 'verse': 'Ezek.11.17'}

**Reading from the Hebrew witness** — support, severity ?.

> Every Masoretic fact this row asserts holds on the witness. The same shape is drawn as an internal parent seam at MT 3:27/4:1, where the mark behind the sub-onset is only a setumah; here it is a petuchah, and setumah and petuchah are not equal. **Resolution.**

- On the second reading of this passage: The two lanes agree on a cut at web:Ezek.21.14 = oshb:Ezek.21.19 and on high not surviving. I accept that high cannot stand and propose high -> medium_low. I do NOT endorse the cut: on the documents the span question is not decided, and I name what decides it. Two correlated Anthropic-family lanes agreeing, with my agreement as a third correlated voice, is exactly the situation the brief tells me not to treat as confirmation, so I went to the strategy's own expected row set - which neither lane cited.
- From the witness: MEASURED for the cut: MT 21:18 is in the 81-verse utterance list and pmarks records PE after it, and that PE is the only mark anywhere inside MT 21:13-22 (the chapter's marks are on MT 21:5, 21:10, 21:12, 21:18, 21:22, 21:28, 21:29, 21:30, 21:32, 21:37). MT 21:19 is one of the 23 ve'atah ben adam verses and the only member of that form inside MT 21:13-22; MT 21:14 and MT 21:17 carry the plain post-posed vocative, and 23 + 70 = 93 reproduces the inventory's son-of-man count exactly. So the row's continuity ground - that the address at MT 21:19 'renews the same address ... now a third time' - blends two device classes and is measurably wrong. §2a line 63 makes a mid-unit utterance 'paragraph-final, not row-final, unless a fresh onset follows', and §6 Q3 lines 320-323 makes 'and you, son of man when it starts a new command' a DEFAULT onset; MT 21:19 is exactly that, so the strategy's own rules put a seam there. MEASURED against the cut, and neither lane raised either point: §3 lines 212-216 prints the zone's expected row set and names web:Ezek.21.8-Ezek.21.17 = oshb:Ezek.21.13-Ezek.21.22 as ONE row, 'the sword song', exactly as shipped, and §7 does not list this span among its held questions - so the cut departs from a named expectation rather than correcting a drift. §6 line 329 sets the target at 7-10 verses and a standing review requirement confirms the 14-16 allowance is for chs 40-42, 45 and 48 only; the shipped row is 10 verses, at the top of the target, and the cut yields 6 and 4, so the reading from the English and structural apparatus's statement that '6 and 4 as two, all inside the ruled band' is false against the strategy's own target. And the corpus itself does not cut at MT 21:24, also a ve'atah ben adam new command, which sits inside Ezekiel 21:18-24 - so in this corpus's own practice the form is not sufficient. the duty to weigh a competing division forbids the PE from making the cut by itself. The evidence therefore does not decide the span. What would decide it: a ruling on whether §3's named expected row set governs over §6 Q3's default-onset rule at a ve'atah ben adam command inside a word-event unit - the same conflict the corpus resolves the other way at MT 21:24. What the evidence does decide is the grade: a high cannot stand over an interior seam that carries the row's only parashah mark, a refrain-grade close and the span's only default-onset form, while the prose denies there is a competing onset at all.
- On audit: CONFIRM
- Adjudicated: ADOPTED — medium_low (proposed: high→medium_low (a reader of a later round))

### Ezekiel 24:15-27

*Recorded confidence: medium. Severity of the disagreement as the reviewer graded it: high.*

**Reading from the English and the structural apparatus** — challenge, severity high.

> The row rejects a seam at MT 24:24/24:25 on the stated ground that no formula device, and it lists word-event, messenger formula, transport and dateline, opens at MT 24:25. That denial is false on the bytes: MT 24:25 opens וְאַתָּ֣ה בֶן אָדָ֔ם, the 'and you, son of man' address, which is itself an onset device, the one that opens a new command where no word-event stands, and the address list holds MT 24:25. The denial is a comma-separated list of four device classes that silently excludes the class actually present. Two further facts belong to the same seam and are undisclosed: a samekh is recorded after MT 24:24, standing exactly on the rival seam, and the close on the other side of the row's own onset, the pe after MT 24:14, is cited as a verse without its mark. The rival now carries evidence on both sides, so the high confidence cannot rest on this reasoning.

Evidence on each side, as this reader set it out:

> For a cut at MT 24:24/24:25: MT 24:24 ends on the plural recognition clause, refrain-grade close evidence, and a samekh is recorded after it; MT 24:25 then opens on the 'and you, son of man' address, an onset device in its own right, and turns from the people addressed through MT 24:21-24 back to the prophet. Against a cut: אֲשֶׁר stands byte-identical at MT 24:24 and MT 24:27, a device straddling the proposed seam and binding the sign to its own payoff; MT 24:26-27 is the mouth-opening promise that answers the muteness laid on the prophet earlier in the book; and the span is bounded hard behind by the word-event formula at MT 24:15. The span should be held at MT 24:15-24:27 on that continuity, which the row does not cite, rather than on a device denial the bytes refute, and the confidence must come down while the seam is open.

What this reader proposed: {'fact': "MT 24:25 opens on the 'and you, son of man' address, so a device does stand at that verse; a samekh is recorded after MT 24:24; and the continuity that holds the span is the sign word standing byte-identical at MT 24:24 and MT 24:27", 'verse': 'oshb:Ezek.24.25, with oshb:Ezek.24.24 and oshb:Ezek.24.27'}

**Reading from the Hebrew witness** — challenge, severity high.

> Two Masoretic facts on the seam this row rejects are missing from it, and together they are the strongest evidence against the row's own confidence. First, a setumah stands at MT 24:24: the row cites that verse five times, as the sign naming, as a recognition-pattern close and as paragraph-final, and never names the mark or its kind, so a setumah is reported only by allusion and never distinguished from a petuchah. Second, the row's ground for refusing a seam at MT 24:24/24:25 enumerates the devices that do not open MT 24:25 and omits the one that does: MT 24:25 opens “וְאַתָּ֣ה בֶן אָדָ֔ם”, the sub-onset that begins a fresh command without an onset formula, and the verse is a member of the 93-verse divine-address list. So the configuration at MT 24:24/24:25 is exactly the shape this corpus treats as a live seam candidate, a refrain-grade close with a mark followed by that sub-onset. The row's span can stand, but its confidence cannot: the same configuration one chapter earlier, a setumah behind at MT 23:35 with a fresh address at MT 23:36, is held at medium in this very part, and the same standard applied here yields medium. Separately, and as the triage asks: the petuchah at MT 24:14 is not disclosed by this row either, which cites that verse for its two closing formulae only; it corroborates the onset, so it changes nothing but the disclosure. The row's positive facts are all true: MT 24:15 is quoted whole and belongs to both the 39-verse strict and 41-verse any-form counts, MT 24:27 is quoted whole and is chapter 24's only member of the 28-verse strict recognition list with a setumah recorded on it, the sign naming at MT 24:24 is an exact prefix, the two strokes at MT 24:17 and MT 24:21 are in the paseq layer, and no variant site falls inside MT 24:15-27.

Evidence on each side, as this reader set it out:

Against a seam: the sign word “לְמוֹפֵ֔ת” at MT 24:24 recurs as “וְהָיִ֤יתָ” at MT 24:27, binding the two ends of the span together; MT 24:27 carries the strict third-person recognition formula and a setumah that close the whole collection; and no dated, strict-onset or messenger device opens MT 24:25, so the sub-onset alone sits below hard-seam grade and a three-verse tail would fall under the granularity target. I therefore keep the span and move the confidence, rather than cutting.

What this reader proposed: {'fact': 'a setumah stands at MT 24:24 and the sub-onset that opens a fresh command without an onset formula stands at MT 24:25, so the rejected seam carries both a marked refrain-grade close and an onset-side device; the petuchah at MT 24:14 behind this onset also needs naming', 'verse': 'Ezek.24.25'}

**Resolution.**

MEASURED from pmarks_Ezek.json: SAMEKH on MT 24:24, standing exactly at the rejected cut, and the row cites MT 24:24 five times and names no mark and no mark kind; PE on MT 24:14, the close behind this row's onset, cited by the row as a verse for its two closing formulae without its mark. MEASURED: MT 24:24 is in NEITHER recognition list, and a recorded input discrepancy names MT 24:24 as one of the five divine-name-pair recognition verses the inventory omits, to be treated as refrain-grade close evidence at tier MEASURED - which makes the rival seam a MARKED refrain-grade close followed by an onset-side address device, and therefore stronger than either lane argued. MEASURED in the row's favour: MT 24:15 is in word_event_formula (39) and word_event_vayehi_any (41); MT 24:27 is in recognition_formula (28) and is chapter 24's only member of it, with SAMEKH recorded on it; the two paseq occurrences the row discloses, MT 24:17 and MT 24:21, are both in the record; and no variant site falls inside MT 24:15-27, the chapter's only one being MT 24:2.

### Ezekiel 32:17-32

*Recorded confidence: medium_low. Severity of the disagreement as the reviewer graded it: high.*

**Reading from the English and the structural apparatus** — challenge, severity high.

> The onset gloss gives MT 32:17 a month that neither witness carries. The dateline itself is sound, since a year word together with a day term is what the dateline category requires, so the defect is in the gloss and the interval drawn from it, not in the seam.

Evidence on each side, as this reader set it out:

> Onset side: MT 32:16 closes the preceding unit on its genre colophon with the utterance signature and an open-section mark at that verse; MT 32:17 is a year-bearing dateline in the perfect word-event form, followed by the command to the prophet at MT 32:18, so the seam stands whatever is decided about the month. Close side: MT 32:32 carries the second of two consecutive utterance signatures, MT 32:31 the first, with an open-section mark at MT 32:32, which is this collection's own close and the strongest close-side evidence in the cluster. Interior: the roster from MT 32:22 to MT 32:30 is a refrain list and is not cut inside, the span carries no messenger formula at all, and the paseq at MT 32:24 is count-only, so no interior candidate arises. On the mirroring flag: the sixteen verses argued but unmirrored are the whole preceding unit cited as a range, and the genre contrast the row draws rests on exactly two of them, the lament word in the opening command and the same word in the colophon, so a two-verse anchor carries the identical argument with fourteen fewer unmirrored references.

What this reader proposed: {'fact': "this onset's date carries a year and a day of the month and names no month, so no interval between it and the preceding dated oracle is determinable from the witnesses", 'verse': 'oshb:Ezek.32.17, against oshb:Ezek.32.1 which names its month'}

**Reading from the Hebrew witness** — challenge, severity medium.

> MT Ezek.32.17 carries a year term and a day-of-the-month term and no month ordinal, so the row's dateline reading of a twelfth month at that verse, and the half-month interval it computes from it, are not on the bytes; the month is supplied only by the preceding dated onset at Ezek.32.1, which does carry a month term. The onset itself is unaffected, because the dated-oracle class is a year word together with a month or a day term, so a month-less dateline is a recognised member of it.

Evidence on each side, as this reader set it out:

> Onset side: Ezek.32.17 is a genuine dated hard-seam onset, with the year term and the day term standing there beside the word-event formula in its perfect form and the prophet-address with the wail imperative following at Ezek.32.18, so correcting the month costs the row no onset evidence. Close side: the roster of the fallen runs to Ezek.32.30 and is not cut inside, the utterance signature is doubled at Ezek.32.31 and Ezek.32.32, and a petuchah follows Ezek.32.32 at the close of the collection. **Resolution.**

- From the witness: The lanes agree here, so I went to the witness. MEASURED from Ezek_oshb.txt with the pointing stripped, Ezek.32.17 reads a year word and then a day-of-the-month term with no month ordinal at all, while Ezek.32.1 carries an explicit twelfth-month term; MEASURED from Ezek_web_clean.txt the translation reads the same way, naming a year and a fifteenth day and no month, against an explicit twelfth month at Ezek.32.1. The row’s gloss "year 12, month 12, day 15 -- a half-month after 32:1’s date" therefore asserts one component the verse does not carry and then computes an interval from it. a recorded input discrepancy names this very verse, so the ruling and the bytes agree. The seam is untouched: MEASURED Ezek.32.17 is 1 of the 7 hayah-perfect word-event verses and 1 of the 14 dated oracles, so a month-less dateline is a full member of the onset class. LF grades this high and OL medium; I would grade it high, because a false factual component in an onset gloss is the kind of claim a downstream consumer cannot check. NEW, filed by neither lane: the row’s boundary_rationale contains the six-word run "wail for the multitude of Egypt", which my sweep MEASURED as identical to the WEB at Ezek.32.18 - the very verse the clause cites. It carries no quotation marks at all and no in-field web: reference, so it reads as the reviewer’s own prose. Under a standing review requirement, five or more consecutive words identical to the WEB at the cited verse are a WEB quotation regardless of delimiter and owe the double curly quotes and an in-field web: reference. The web_quotes member reported zero for my whole group. Everything else in this row verifies true: MEASURED PE on Ezek.32.16 at the onset seam and PE on Ezek.32.32 at the close seam, both disclosed in the right direction, and no mark at all inside Ezek.32.17-Ezek.32.31, so there is no undisclosed interior mark; MEASURED one paseq occurrence at Ezek.32.24 and ketiv/qere sites at Ezek.32.31 and Ezek.32.32, all disclosed; MEASURED utterance verses at Ezek.32.31 and Ezek.32.32, so the doubled close is real; and MEASURED the wail verb occurs at Ezek.32.18 and nowhere else in the book, while the qinah noun stands at Ezek.32.2 and Ezek.32.16 and nowhere inside this span, which is exactly the contrast the row discloses and which the pinned strategy licenses for this span with disclosure.

### Ezekiel 42:15-20

*Recorded confidence: medium_low. Severity of the disagreement as the reviewer graded it: high.*

**Reading from the English and the structural apparatus** — challenge, severity medium.

> The ketiv/qere at MT 42:16 falls on the numeral word itself and not beside it. The ketiv is the measure noun for cubits and the qere is the numeral for hundreds, while the other three sides at 42:17, 42:18 and 42:19 all read five hundred. The variant therefore governs whether the east side measures five or five hundred, and the stated reading that it is a lexical variant which is not a numeral and does not affect the reed count does not hold. The generalization that the chapter-40 ketiv/qere forms stand beside a number and never on it is true of that cluster and does not reach MT 42:16.

Evidence on each side, as this reader set it out:

> The boundary is unaffected and holds from both sides. Close side at MT 42:20: the verse ends on the purpose of the wall, לְהַבְדִּ֕יל בֵּ֥ין הַקֹּ֖דֶשׁ לְחֹֽל, which is a terminal clause and rests on no disputed reading. Onset side at MT 43:1: the guide transport with the glory approaching behind it opens a different device family from measurement. What the variant does bear on is the unit internal shape, which is the evidence that 42:15-42:20 is a single movement: under the qere the four sides are one symmetrical five-hundred circuit, and under the ketiv the east side alone would break that symmetry.

What this reader proposed: {'fact': 'at MT 42:16 the ketiv is the measure noun and the qere is the numeral for hundreds, and the three parallel sides at MT 42:17, 42:18 and 42:19 read five hundred', 'verse': 'oshb:Ezek.42.16'}

**Reading from the Hebrew witness** — challenge, severity high.

> Two Masoretic facts are wrong. First and materially: the row clears the ketiv/qere at MT 42:16 as a spelling variant that is not a numeral and does not affect the reed count, but the read form there is the plural of the hundred-word, the multiplier itself, against a written form that is the cubit-word. The three parallel perimeter verses MT 42:17, MT 42:18 and MT 42:19 and the summary at MT 42:20 all carry the hundred-word plainly, so the variant at MT 42:16 is exactly a numeral variant and it is the magnitude of that one perimeter figure that turns on it. The quotation the row binds to MT 42:16 for its measure verb also carries no ketiv/qere disclosure alongside it. Second: the row states that each of the four cardinal measurements opens with a measure verb, which holds at MT 42:16 and MT 42:17 but not at MT 42:18, which opens with the object marker and puts the verb after the direction, nor at MT 42:19, which opens with a turning verb and puts the measure verb fourth.

Evidence on each side, as this reader set it out:

> Onset: MT 42:15 pairs a completion notice with a transport verb, which is a close-grade device pressed into onset service, and the row says so. Close: MT 42:20 ends on the separating purpose of the wall, and MT 43:1 opposite carries a transport verb and opens the glory material. Neither side has any paragraph-mark corroboration, because the book carries no mark between the open section at MT 39:29 and the closed section at MT 43:9. The seam itself is well supported from both sides and stands; what fails is the clearance given to the textual variant inside the row, and a measurement row that reports a numeral variant as not a numeral is the one error a downstream consumer will take as already checked.

What this reader proposed: {'fact': 'The ketiv/qere at MT 42:16 is a numeral-bearing variant: the read form is the plural hundred-word and the written form is the cubit-word, so the magnitude of the east-side perimeter figure turns on it, and the three parallel verses MT 42:17-42:19 together with the summary at MT 42:20 carry the hundred-word without a variant. The verse also needs its ketiv/qere disclosed alongside the measure-verb quotation, and the clause-initial measure verb holds only at MT 42:16 and MT 42:17.', 'verse': 'oshb:Ezek.42.16'}

**Resolution.**

- On the second reading of this passage: Both lanes challenge, both are MEASURED right, and OL/c20's higher severity is the correct one. The seam itself stands from both sides. Span stands; confidence stands.
- From the witness: MEASURED: pmarks kq['Ezek.42.16'] is the pair אמות / מֵא֥וֹת - the written form is the cubit word and the READ form is the plural of the hundred word, the multiplier itself. The running text reads חֲמֵשׁ אמות קָנִ֛ים, so under the qere the east side measures five hundred reeds and under the ketiv it does not. The three parallel sides carry the hundred word plainly - Ezek.42.17, 42:18 and 42:19 all read חֲמֵשׁ מֵא֥וֹת קָנִים - and the summary at 42:20 carries חֲמֵ֣שׁ מֵא֔וֹת twice. The row's boundary_evidence_refs[1] and device_notes both clear that variant as 'a lexical spelling variant not affecting the reed-count' and 'not a numeral'. That is false on the bytes, and in a measurement row it is the error a downstream consumer will treat as already checked, so I concur with OL/c20's higher severity. Second MEASURED defect, found by both lanes: 'each opening with a measure verb on its own line' holds at 42:16 and 42:17 only - Ezek.42.18 opens אֵ֛ת ר֥וּחַ הַדָּר֖וֹם with מָדָ֑ד fourth, and Ezek.42.19 opens סָבַ֖ב with מָדַ֛ד fourth. Neither defect moves the seam: Ezek.42.20 closes on לְהַבְדִּ֕יל בֵּ֥ין הַקֹּ֖דֶשׁ לְחֹֽל with no disputed reading involved, Ezek.42.15 pairs the completion notice וְכִלָּ֗ה with the transport verb וְהוֹצִיאַ֨נִי֙ in one verse, the strategy names 42:20/43:1 a hard seam, and the two paseq occurrences (42:15, 42:20) and the mark absence are exact. ONE ITEM I REPORT RATHER THAN SCORE: Ezek.42.15 is not in the strategy's enumerated 20-verse transport class although the same strategy names it as a transport-verb cut site - the same intra-input disagreement as at 40:48, routed upward on the a standing review requirement principle. The row's own ketiv quotation, 'אמות (oshb:Ezek.42.16, ketiv)', is labelled, slash-free and an exact substring of the running text: a standing review requirement compliant.
- On audit: CONFIRM

Second fact in the same row: the rival interior cuts are dismissed as plain lineated continuations at both candidate points, and the translation changes texture at both — lineation ends at web:Ezek.27.11 and web:Ezek.27.12 begins prose behind a paragraph break, and the prose list ends at web:Ezek.27.24 with lineation resuming at web:Ezek.27.25. That layer should carry no weight in a rival rejection at all, and here the mis-stated version of it is what makes the rejection read clean.

Close side: MT 27:36 carries no member of the closing formula families and rests on the setumah after it plus its own terminal clause “בַּלָּה֣וֹת הָיִ֔ית וְאֵינֵ֖ךְ עַד עוֹלָֽם”, with a fresh word-event onset at MT 28:1 ahead. Second, the lament noun recurs inside the span at MT 27:32 (וְנָשְׂא֨וּ אֵלַ֤יִךְ בְּנִיהֶם֙ קִינָ֔ה וְקוֹנְנ֖וּ), in the same reported-speech shape the row contrasts its opening command with at MT 26:17 (וְנָשְׂא֨וּ עָלַ֤יִךְ קִינָה֙), which lies outside the span, while the in-span instance goes unmentioned. A third ground the row gives against internal cuts at MT 27:11/27:12 and at MT 27:25/27:26 is a poetic-line layout feature rather than a textual one; the checkable grounds at those two points are that the mark record carries nothing there and no formula stands in either verse, and both of those are true. Across all six rows of this cluster every reference the row does carry as boundary evidence lies inside its own span and every flagged one lies outside it, so the flags track cross-span neighbour and comparison citation, not a missing ground.
