Within this same part, Ezekiel 43:18-27's close verse Ezek.43.27 carries two consecutive SAMEKH - the book's only doubled mark - and Ezekiel 43:18-27's own prose calls that 'the best-attested close available'. The two rows therefore contradict each other on one object and the bytes decide it against Ezekiel 43:1-9; a scope word must be narrowed or 43:27 named, with no span or confidence consequence. ONE ITEM I REPORT RATHER THAN SCORE: the row's onset calls Ezek.43.1's וַיּוֹלִכֵ֖נִי a transport verb, and MEASURED it is not a member of the strategy's enumerated 20-verse class (43:5 is). Both lanes called it a transport verb too. Same class-boundary item as 40:24, 40:48 and 42:15 - reported upward, no row scored. The a standing review requirement defect is Ezek.43.6: in-span, argued three times, unmirrored, on a row to which NO refs_mirror flag was attributed at all.
- On audit: CONFIRM

### Ezekiel 43:18-27

*Recorded confidence: medium. Severity of the disagreement as the reviewer graded it: low. The two initial readings reached different conclusions.*

**Reading from the English and the structural apparatus** — challenge, severity low.

> MT 43:18 opens with the guide speech, וַיֹּ֣אמֶר אֵלַ֗י, before the son-of-man address and the messenger formula. It is one of the thirty-six verses in the book carrying that speech form, and inside a vision a unit discloses the speech verse it opens on. The onset account names the address and the messenger formula but not the speech verb, and states that the verse opens on the address, which is not what stands at its head. The same device is treated as boundary-grade at MT 42:13 and deliberately contrasted against the other speech root at MT 41:22, so the omission is inconsistent within one stretch of text.

Evidence on each side, as this reader set it out:

> The boundary is right and the correction only strengthens it. Onset side at MT 43:18: three devices stack in one verse - the guide speech, the son-of-man address and the messenger formula - and the material turns from the altar dimensions to the altar ordinances. Close side at MT 43:27: the utterance signature ends the unit, אֶתְכֶ֔ם נְאֻ֖ם אֲדֹנָ֥י יְהֹוִֽה, and two consecutive samekh marks follow it on that verse, the one verse in the book carrying two of them, single witness; the utterance at 43:19 is paragraph-final inside the unit because the seven-day instructions run on past it through 43:25 and 43:26. On the far side MT 44:1 opens with its own transport.

What this reader proposed: {'fact': 'MT 43:18 opens with the guide speech verb, ahead of the son-of-man address and the messenger formula', 'verse': 'oshb:Ezek.43.18'}

**Reading from the Hebrew witness** — support, severity ?.

> Every Masoretic fact in this row is true on the bytes. The divine address and the messenger formula both stand at MT 43:18, which is one of the ninety-three address verses and one of the hundred and twenty-two messenger-formula verses; the utterance signature stands mid-verse at MT 43:19 and verse-final at MT 43:27, and the row quotes both with their differing pointing of the divine name rather than levelling them; the ketiv/qere at MT 43:26 is a number variant on the body-part noun inside the consecration idiom and is not a measurement; the corpus records two consecutive closed-section marks at MT 43:27, the only doubled paragraph mark in the book, counted as two occurrences on one verse and kept out of the verse total; and neither a paseq nor an editorial note falls anywhere in MT 43:18-43:27.

Evidence on each side, as this reader set it out:

> Onset: MT 43:18 carries the divine address plus the messenger formula, a speech onset after the throne declaration closes at MT 43:9. Close: MT 43:27 ends on the acceptance clause and the utterance signature and is the one verse in the book carrying two consecutive paragraph marks, so the close has the best Masoretic attestation available anywhere in this cluster. MT 44:1 opposite opens with its own return verb and no continuation particle, so the seam does not depend on reading the eighth-day horizon as complete. Both sides converge; the middle confidence band stands and is arguably understated.

**Resolution.**

- On the second reading of this passage: LF/c20's challenge stands and OL/c20's support does NOT. This is the one row in my two clusters where a support verdict conceals a measurable falsehood, and OL's blanket 'every Masoretic fact in this row is true on the bytes' is itself the overclaim. Span and confidence stand.
- From the witness: MEASURED from Ezek_oshb.txt: Ezek.43.18 reads וַיֹּ֣אמֶר אֵלַ֗י בֶּן אָדָם֙ כֹּ֤ה אָמַר֙ אֲדֹנָ֣י יְהוִ֔ה ... The verse OPENS with the say-form וַיֹּ֣אמֶר אֵלַ֗י; the son-of-man address is second and the messenger formula third. The WEB at 43:18 agrees, opening 'He said to me'. The row states that '43:18 opens with בֶּן אָדָם֙ כֹּ֤ה אָמַר֙ אֲדֹנָ֣י יְהוִ֔ה', which is false as to what stands at the verse head, and it never discloses the speech verb at all - although Ezek.43.18 is one of the ויאמר אלי verses the strategy sweeps, although a speech verse inside a vision is a seam-grade device by the strategy's own granularity rule, and although the sibling rows Ezekiel 42:1-14 and Ezekiel 41:12-26 both treat that exact form as boundary-grade and contrast it against the speaking root. So the omission is inconsistent inside one stretch of text as well as wrong on the verse. LF/c20 stands at MEDIUM. OL/c20's enumerated facts are each true - 43:18 is a member of the 93 and of the 122; the utterance signature stands mid-verse at 43:19 and verse-final at 43:27 with the divine name pointed differently in the two; the kq at 43:26 is a number variant on יד/ו and not a measurement; Ezek.43.27 carries two consecutive SAMEKH, the only doubled mark among the 183 marked verses, correctly counted as two occurrences on one verse; neither paseq nor editorial note falls in the span - but the UNIVERSAL that frames them is not, and a support verdict that certifies every fact is the one shape of error a peer round exists to catch. The correction strengthens the boundary rather than weakening it: three devices stack in Ezek.43.18 instead of two. The a standing review requirement defects are Ezek.44.1 (in-window) and Ezek.43.25 (in-span, invisible to the validator).
- On audit: CONFIRM

### Ezekiel 44:1-4

*Recorded confidence: medium_low. Severity of the disagreement as the reviewer graded it: low.*

**Reading from the English and the structural apparatus** — support, severity ?.

> Ezek.44.1-44.4 holds one literary movement and not two: the guide's movement verb at 44.1 reopens the vision frame at the shut outer east gate, 44.2-3 give the gate's meaning and the prince's privilege, and 44.4 is a second movement of the same guided scene, ending on the glory filling the house and the prostration that is this book's vision-close gesture, borne by 5 verses in all (1.28, 3.23, 11.13, 43.3, 44.4). The competing division that would attach 44.4 to the statute is weaker because 44.4 carries no statute diction and the instructional mode does not begin until the address at 44.5.

Evidence on each side, as this reader set it out:

> Close side of the row's own seam: 44.4 ends on the prostration after the glory is seen, and there is no statute language anywhere in 44.1-44.4. Onset side: 44.5 opens with the address to the son of man and the directive to attend to the ordinances and entrances of the house, which changes the mode from narrated vision to statute; it is one of only 2 son-of-man addresses in chs 44-48 (44.5, 47.6) per the device census. Behind the row's own onset, the witness records a doubled setumah on 43.27, so the opening seam is marked on its far side as well. The row argues from 43.27 in prose without mirroring it in its structured evidence list; this does not damage the form argument, because the onset carries its own cited device at 44.1.

What this reader proposed: {'fact': 'no boundary change; optionally mirror the closing mark behind the onset in the structured evidence, since the prose already argues from it', 'verse': 'Ezek.43.27'}

**Reading from the Hebrew witness** — challenge, severity low.

> The four Hebrew quotations of this row collate byte-for-byte at Ezek.44.1, Ezek.44.3, Ezek.44.4 and Ezek.44.5, and the seam devices are all real on the bytes; but the supporting claim that the prostration close matches the vision inclusio at Ezek.1.28, Ezek.3.23, Ezek.11.13 and Ezek.43.3 is offered without the two qualifiers the witness requires. The fell-on-the-face verb stands in six verses of the book - Ezek.1.28, Ezek.3.23, Ezek.9.8, Ezek.11.13, Ezek.43.3 and Ezek.44.4 - so Ezek.9.8 is missing from a list presented as the matching set; and of the four named, only Ezek.43.3 shares Ezek.44.4's preposition before the face noun, the other three carrying the other preposition. A second, smaller gap: Ezek.44.3 carries two non-variant editorial notes and only one is disclosed.

Evidence on each side, as this reader set it out:

> Onset side: Ezek.44.1 carries the returning-transport verb, one of only three occurrences of that form in the whole vision block (Ezek.44.1, Ezek.47.1, Ezek.47.6), and the utterance signature together with a pair of setumoth stands at Ezek.43.27 immediately before it; the ruled strategy also treats the chapter division at Ezek.44.1 as coinciding with a default onset. Close side: Ezek.44.4 carries the bring-me transport verb, one of seven in the vision block, plus the prostration; Ezek.44.5 carries the son-of-man address, which the book's structural counts place at Ezek.44.5 and then nowhere again until Ezek.47.6. The seam has a device on each side, the span stands and the medium_low confidence stands; only the inclusio list and the note disclosure need correcting.

What this reader proposed: {'fact': "The fell-on-the-face prostration verb stands in six verses of the book, the sixth carrying the lengthened form of the verb, and Ezek.44.4's preposition before the face noun matches only one of the other vision occurrences; separately, the verse carrying the anomalous-form note also carries a second non-variant editorial note.", 'verse': 'Ezek.9.8 (the omitted sixth prostration), Ezek.43.3 (the one preposition-matching occurrence), Ezek.44.3 (the second editorial note)'}

**Resolution.**

- On the second reading of this passage: LF's reading stands: the span 44:1-44:4 and the medium_low confidence keep. OL's primary ground fails on the bytes and under ruling a standing review requirement. Two defects neither lane named stand against the row, both disclosure-grade.
- From the witness: MEASURED, my own consonantal-skeleton sweep of Ezek_oshb.txt: the prostration form used at 44:4 stands in exactly 5 verses - 1:28, 3:23, 11:13, 43:3, 44:4 - member for member identical both to the five-verse class the pinned strategy declares and to the list this row prints. the reading from the Hebrew witness challenged the row for omitting 9:8 as a sixth member; 9:8 carries a DIFFERENT form (the lengthened one), which my sweep returns in exactly 1 verse. Ruling a standing review requirement governs: a sweep class contains exactly the verses its list names, and a lane finding a candidate member reports it against the inventory, not against the row. OL widened a declared class by prose and then charged the row for the omission, which is what a standing review requirement forbids. OL's second observation is true on the bytes but is a refinement, not a defect: MEASURED, 43:3 and 44:4 carry one preposition before the face noun while 1:28, 3:23, 9:8 and 11:13 carry the other, yet the row asserts membership in the prostration inclusio and never asserts preposition identity. AGAINST the row, and named by neither lane: the requirement to disclose a section mark in each direction it bears on measured in all three directions gives onset seam Ezek.43.27 = SAMEKH + SAMEKH, interior = none, close seam Ezek.44.4 = none. The double samekh at 43:27 is independently corroborated by the strategy's own parashah paragraph, which records 43:27 as the single verse carrying two consecutive samekh segs. The row's device_notes then states that its span 'carries no parashah corroboration on either side'. Under ruling a standing review requirement a mark on the verse BEFORE the first verse stands at the onset seam, so the 43:27 double samekh IS this row's onset-seam corroboration - and the row's own boundary_rationale argues from it. The clause is a false absence claim in the onset direction and contradicts the row's own rationale. Both lanes checked only the adjacent narrow sentence, which my measurement confirms TRUE: no mark stands on any of 44:1 through 44:13.
- On audit: CONFIRM

### Ezekiel 48:30-35

*Recorded confidence: medium_low. Severity of the disagreement as the reviewer graded it: low.*

**Reading from the English and the structural apparatus** — challenge, severity low.

> The close evidence asserts that no mark can be recorded after MT 48:35 because it is the book's final verse; what the staged evidence supports is the measured absence - the mark inventory's last entry for this book is the petuchah after MT 48:29 and it records nothing after MT 48:35 - and not the impossibility, which is a claim about the recording convention rather than about the text.

Evidence on each side, as this reader set it out:

> Onset side: nothing formulaic opens MT 48:30, and the seam behind it is carried by the utterance signature in MT 48:29 with the petuchah recorded after it, plus the turn from the portions summary to the city's exits and gates. Close side: the book ends at MT 48:35, and the row is right that no mark is recorded there and that one paseq occurrence is listed in that verse. The span holds one movement - four sides, twelve gates, the circumference and the name - and the ruled vocabulary assigns this coda to the allotment class, so the span and its class stand; only the warrant's wording overreaches.

What this reader proposed: {'fact': "state the measured absence - the mark inventory's last entry for this book is the petuchah after MT 48:29 and it records nothing after MT 48:35 - in place of the assertion that no mark is possible there", 'verse': 'Ezek.48.35'}

**Reading from the Hebrew witness** — challenge, severity low.

> The absent mark after MT 48:35 is given a reason, that none is possible on the book's last verse, and the witness does not support that reason. Marks stand freely on chapter-final verses here: 39 of the book's 48 chapter-final verses carry one, and MT 43:27 carries two consecutive closed-section marks. What the witness supports is only that none is recorded after MT 48:35.

Evidence on each side, as this reader set it out:

> MT 48:29 side: the utterance signature and an open-section mark stand in the same verse and close the portion list; that is true, though it is argued in prose without appearing among the row's own cited refs. MT 48:30 side: no mark stands there, and the verse opens with the same heading form as MT 48:1, so the seam rests on the turn to the city's gates. The span is right on the bytes, with four sides measured and three gates each at MT 48:30-34 and the circumference and new name at MT 48:35, and the paseq inside MT 48:35 is recorded and correctly held to a count with no position claimed. Only the stated reason for the closing absence needs narrowing to what is recorded.

What this reader proposed: {'fact': 'no parashah mark is recorded after MT 48:35 in this witness, and marks do stand on chapter-final verses here, including two consecutive closed-section marks at MT 43:27', 'verse': 'Ezek.48.35'}

**Resolution.**

- On the second reading of this passage: Both lanes challenged at low on the same ground and my own measurement confirms it and supplies the decisive figure. The span and the medium_low confidence stand. One a standing review requirement defect stands that the web_quotes member did not report, and one a standing review requirement instance is where the scoping file's split and the a standing review requirement rule disagree - I apply the rule.
- From the witness: MEASURED from pmarks_Ezek.json and verse_inventory.json: 39 of the book's 48 chapter-final verses carry a parashah mark, and the nine that do not are 8:18, 10:22, 13:23, 20:49, 40:49, 41:26, 42:20, 46:24 and 48:35. the reading from the Hebrew witness's 39-of-48 figure is exact. So the row's statement that 'no parashah mark is recorded after it, since none is possible on the book's last verse' asserts an impossibility the witness contradicts twice over: marks stand freely on chapter-final verses here, and eight other chapter-final verses also lack one, so the absence at 48:35 is ordinary rather than structural. What the witness supports is only the measured absence, which is true. GENUINE, LOW - the wording overreaches while the fact holds - and both lanes are right. Everything else measures true. MEASURED from pmarks: onset seam 48:29 = PE and the row discloses it as its onset warrant, which is the correct a standing review requirement direction for a mark on the verse before the first verse; interior 48:30-48:34 = no mark; close seam 48:35 = no mark. MEASURED: the span carries exactly one paseq occurrence, at 48:35, and the row discloses it count-only with no position asserted, satisfying ruling a standing review requirement and respecting ruling a standing review requirement. MEASURED by my own ketiv sweep: no variant site inside the span, so ruling a standing review requirement owes nothing, confirming both lanes. MEASURED from my own byte sweep of ch 48: no messenger formula and no son-of-man address anywhere in the chapter, so the row's no-formula-onset claim at 48:30 is true. MEASURED against the strategy: its temple-vision entry names '48:30-35 the gates and the name - the book's close without a formula' and its land-allotment unit type names 48:30-35, so this span is declared exactly as the row draws it, and it is the one row of my four in ch 48 whose seams coincide with the witness's own sectioning on both sides.
- On audit: CONFIRM

## 4. What changed as a result of the review

The most consequential fact about this review is what it did not change. Across all 145 units, the later readings proposed no change to any unit boundary: no boundary change was proposed. The disagreements recorded above are therefore overwhelmingly about the STATED GROUNDS for a boundary rather than about where the boundary falls — about whether the reason given is true to the witness, whether a competing division was weighed, and whether a device the text carries was disclosed. A reader who expected a review of this size to move boundaries should read that as the finding it is.

Confidence was re-graded on a number of units where the recorded grade overstated or understated the evidence. In every such case the grade moved to match the witness, and no unit's confidence was raised without a reviewer having asked for it.

## 5. What remains open

The following are recorded as unsettled. They are not oversights; each is a question the evidence available here cannot close.

- Whether the pe recorded on MT 3:16 stands inside that verse, between the seven-days clause and the word-event clause, or after it. The staged witness records the mark on the verse and does not carry the placement within it.
- MT 3:15 carries its ketiv/qere at the unpointed word in the middle of the verse, while the clause quoted as the unit's close is a separate, already pointed occurrence at the end of the same verse. The row's note that its own splice reads the qere conflates the two words.
- Every row in this cluster carries one refs_mirror flag for verses argued in prose but absent from the structured evidence list - MT 2:1 on the first row, MT 3:10 and MT 3:14 through MT 4:17 on later ones.
- The triage answers record confidence_high_survives as null rather than true or false.
- Whether Ezek.5.1-Ezek.5.17 is one unit or two, divided at 5:4/5:5 between the commanded action and its interpretation. A pe is recorded after MT 5:4 and the messenger formula opens Ezek.5.5, but no refrain-grade close stands at 5:4, and the clause shared by Ezek.5.2 and Ezek.5.12 binds the interpretation to the action (sweep: 3 verses carry that clause).
- The cut at 7:9/7:10 is licensed by a refrain close with no onset device opposite it. Under the book's granularity rule a unit runs from an onset to the verse before the next onset, and after the messenger formula at Ezek.7.5 the next onset device is the dateline at Ezek.8.1.
- Outside this cluster and not adjudicated here: oshb:Ezek.21.14 (MT 21:14) = web:Ezek.21.9 carries the messenger formula in a short form, with no divine name after 'Lord'. The device census's 122-verse messenger list does not carry that verse, and the short-form note names only the other short form. A consonantal-skeleton sweep of the 'thus says the Lord' sequence over the staged MT extract returns 123 verses against the census's 122, and that verse is the difference.
- Whether Ezek.10.8 closes the coal errand or opens the cherub description. Its notice of a man-shaped hand under the wings recurs at Ezek.10.21, and the under-their-wings phrase stands at three verses (Ezek.1.8, 10.8, 10.21), which would group Ezek.10.8 with the description; against that, Ezek.10.7 names the cherub's own outstretched hand, which Ezek.10.8 accounts for.
- Whether Ezek.10.15 belongs inside a unit characterised as static description: it narrates the cherubim mounting up, the action Ezek.10.18-10.19 completes, while the verses on either side of it describe.
- Whether the samekh on MT 8:14 argues a seam between the Tammuz sighting and the speech at Ezek.8.15 rather than after Ezek.8.15. The tradition's ch-8 division, after MT 8:6 and after MT 8:14, sits one verse out of phase with the again-see pointer at Ezek.8.6, 8.13 and 8.15, and the inventory does not carry where inside a verse a mark falls.
- The lens summary given to this reviewer states 13 oracle datelines and 3 festival-calendar dates, while the device inventory at the digest bound above reads 14 datelines (adding MT 24:1) and 4 calendar dates (adding MT 45:18), both recorded there as corrections dated 2026-09-08.
- Whether the oracle at Ezek.11.14-Ezek.11.21 is a row of its own or a sub-unit held inside an Ezek.11.1-Ezek.11.21 courtyard scene remains a live reading. It is left as its own row here on the strength of its formula bracket, with the confidence lowered rather than the boundary moved.
- The witness divides 11:1 from 11:2 with a pe, which groups 11:1 with what precedes rather than with the courtyard speech, and the staged files do not carry where a mark sits relative to the words of its own verse.
- The onset at 11:22 carries no device from the book's onset vocabulary — it is a resumptive repetition of 10:19 — while the ch-10 scene onsets argued from that same glory device sit one confidence level below the Ezek.11.22-Ezek.11.25 unit.
- Four of this cluster's seven rows carry the near-threshold seven-word disclosure run about the absence of a Qere note and of a paseq; that run stands on eight rows across the corpus against a hard limit of ten.
- Whether the interior candidate between Ezek.13.14 and Ezek.13.15 deserves a recorded held note rather than a disclosure only.
- Whether Ezek.14.1 should travel with the material before it, as the witness's own paragraph division has it, rather than heading the row that answers the elders.
- The retained-low record binds the toolkit at digest 06b0e7a6379b30deff2662009f6b20dd4fbc408333944c95f3111e305f2a57ee, while that file now hashes to b247cea5b0af11a7708b5b0a53383d4b5abd9940b016e045bd1f317d97c1c0b6, the digest pinned for this review.
- None of the six spans in this cluster is wrong, and five of the six confidence levels stand as shipped.
- Whether the seam belongs at 16:22/16:23 or stays at 16:23/16:24. The temporal frame opening 16:23 governs the narrative verb opening 16:24, while the utterance signature ending 16:23 is real close-grade evidence, and the paragraph layer records nothing at either candidate.
- One mark, two readings: the pe recorded as following MT 16:35 is read as a close one verse beyond the 16:24-16:34 row and as onset corroboration by the 16:35-16:43 row.
- Whether a signal key naming a device its span does not contain is a field defect or frame disclosure. Three rows here carry a word-event key with no word-event verse inside the span, which is the mandated shared-frame disclosure for rows sitting inside one word-event unit.
- whether the seam inside the sisters' comparison belongs at 16:50/16:51, where the single paragraph mark of that stretch stands, or at 16:51/16:52, where the bearing-of-shame arc that 16:58 closes begins
- whether a casuistic protasis such as the one opening 18:5 counts as an onset device in this book
- the division of the eagle-riddle complex was carried as an open question, yet three of its four spans ship at high confidence
- The book strategy's sweep for the parable noun counts MT 19:11 among its verses and flags only one homograph elsewhere in that list. On the bytes MT 19:11 is the ruling-root participle in the scepters clause, so that sweep appears to carry a second homograph, and the colophon's infinitive at MT 19:14 is a third form of the same root.
- Where a mark falls inside its verse is not carried by the mark inventory, which records the mark on the verse it follows. For the four closes in this cluster that lean on a mark - MT 18:20, 18:23, 18:26 and 18:32 - I could confirm the mark's type and its verse but not its position within that verse.
- The paseq layer is count-only by the inventory's own statement. I verified that MT 18 records exactly one paseq occurrence, at MT 18:20, and that MT 19 records none; I did not and could not verify any position for it.
- Whether the turn at oshb:Ezek.20.30 from the fathers' record to the present generation counts as the addressee change the book requires before a messenger formula may open a unit. On the wording of the commands it does not: the addressee named at oshb:Ezek.20.27 and at oshb:Ezek.20.30 is the house of Israel in both, and only the subject of the accusation moves.
- Whether the samekh the mark list records after oshb:Ezek.20.1 weighs against reading that verse as a narrative frame whose word-event at oshb:Ezek.20.2 is the oracle's onset inside it. The list gives no position within the verse and the mark is single-witness.
- The mirroring flags on five of my six units name references argued in prose but absent from the boundary-evidence list: oshb:Ezek.19.14 in the unit opening at oshb:Ezek.20.1; oshb:Ezek.20.26 in the unit opening at oshb:Ezek.20.27; web:Ezek.20.49 = oshb:Ezek.21.5 in the unit opening at oshb:Ezek.20.39; the MT-side coordinates of the zone references in the unit opening at web:Ezek.20.45 = oshb:Ezek.21.1; and web:Ezek.20.45 = oshb:Ezek.21.1 together with web:Ezek.20.48 = oshb:Ezek.21.4 in the unit opening at web:Ezek.21.1 = oshb:Ezek.21.6. I verified each of those facts against the mark list, the paseq list and the verse bytes and each is true.
- whether the pe cited at web:Ezek.21.13 = oshb:Ezek.21.18 follows that verse or falls within it
- the unit type of the second span the proposed cut creates, web:Ezek.21.14-Ezek.21.17 = oshb:Ezek.21.19-Ezek.21.22
- whether the sentence in the reviewed row that calls web:Ezek.21.12 = oshb:Ezek.21.17 a carrier of a translation paragraph mark is wrong or only ambiguous
- the historical binding recorded in the retained-lows artifact does not match the live toolkit digest
- Inside MT 23:36-49 a messenger formula stands at MT 23:46 with a samekh recorded after MT 23:45 behind it, a marked close followed by a speech onset, the same shape treated as a seam candidate elsewhere. The row discloses the mark and never names the formula, and its addressee does not change across the pair.
- The automated mark cross-check that produced the two entries handed to this review keys on the verse standing behind a row's onset. The two marks this review found undisclosed, a samekh after MT 23:35 and a samekh after MT 24:24, both lie inside their rows' own spans, so that check would not surface either.
- The second-person recognition category, five verses, is labelled by its masculine form, while the close at MT 22:16 reads the feminine singular; the row wrote 'second-person' in prose and did not repeat the category's label.
- The book's own conventions ask every row to carry its dated block as metadata; the row schema has no field for it, and the five rows in MT 22:1-23:49 that sit under the date opening this collection do not name it in prose either.
- The 'first foreign addressee' wording is not local to one unit: the same priority claim stands in the book-level onset description for this collection, phrased the same way.
- Whether the pe after oshb:Ezek.25.14 and the samekh marks of my span follow their verses or fall inside them cannot be settled from the staged bytes.
- The internal division at oshb:Ezek.25.6 remains a live reading at the form level: a messenger formula there follows a refrain-grade close at oshb:Ezek.25.5 that carries its own samekh, which is one of the two licensed conditions for a cut.
- Both retained low findings were checked and neither is reported as a defect: the hand-of-YHWH form and its count of 7 are consistent because that category matches on the construct alone, and no verse of my span carries the idiom; and the historical artifact binding in the retained-lows record differs from the live toolkit digest by design.
- The book's plan names the Israel coda as a three-verse unit beginning at MT 28:24, and lists the MT 28:23-to-28:24 pair among the onset-and-close collisions whose seam falls after the close verse; the corpus instead cuts after MT 28:24.
- The same plan lists Ezek.26.15-26.21 under both the nation-oracle type and the lament type.
- All six rows carry the argued-but-unmirrored reference flag, as do 115 of the corpus's 145 rows.
- The last row of the cluster calls Ezek 25-28 a closed span.
- inside the dateline block oshb:Ezek.29.1-Ezek.29.16 the witness divides after oshb:Ezek.29.7, oshb:Ezek.29.12 and oshb:Ezek.29.16, while the two units covering the block divide at oshb:Ezek.29.9 / oshb:Ezek.29.10, the one internal boundary there with no recorded mark
- the year-27 date at oshb:Ezek.29.17 is the latest date in the book only within the exile reckoning; the thirtieth-year figure at oshb:Ezek.1.1 belongs to a different system
- the notes of the unit spanning oshb:Ezek.29.17-Ezek.29.21 already carry one of the saturated seven-word runs, the one that denies punctuation-layer marks in a span
- whether the Israel-facing coda at oshb:Ezek.29.21, with its mouth-opening promise, should disclose the arc that runs from oshb:Ezek.3.26 through oshb:Ezek.24.27 to oshb:Ezek.33.22
- The level on the Sheol-roster unit at Ezek.32.17-Ezek.32.32 is the lowest in the cluster, while its boundary evidence is the strongest: a year-bearing dateline onset with an open-section mark and a genre colophon behind it, and a doubled utterance close with an open-section mark that is also this collection's own close. The row's own reasoning ties the level to the held genre call rather than to the boundaries, and the eighteen-verse cedar unit at Ezek.31.1-Ezek.31.18, whose genre call is held in the same way, sits a step higher.
- The length disclosure on the cedar unit at Ezek.31.1-Ezek.31.18 measures itself against the fourteen-to-sixteen verse allowance, which the ruled granularity grants to measured and listed blocks; this unit is neither, and the ruled target density for an ordinary formula-bounded unit is lower.
- Whether the cure for the month at Ezek.32.17 should state that the witnesses name no month, or simply omit the month from the gloss, is a convention question and not a factual one.
- Whether the 'and you, son of man' command opening is meant to count as a row onset in its own right or only as a sub-onset inside a word-event unit. The frame spine calls the son-of-man address onset corroboration and never an onset alone, while the granularity rule lists that command among the onsets a row may be cut at. MT ch 33 carries four of them, at Ezek.33.7, Ezek.33.10, Ezek.33.12 and Ezek.33.30, and this cluster cuts at two and not at the other two.
- Whether the interior site at Ezek.34.19/Ezek.34.20 should be recorded in Ezekiel 34:17-31 as a weighed alternative rather than only as internal texture. I judge that it fails, because the address does not durably change across Ezek.34.20 and Ezek.34.21, but the row never weighs it and its rejected alternative looks only at the merge with the preceding span.
- Where a paragraph mark stands inside its verse. The inventory records only which verse a mark follows, so for every close in this cluster that rests on a mark I could confirm the verse and not the position.
- Whether the two-verse span at Ezek.33.21-Ezek.33.22 is inside or outside the over-split guard. The guard admits a row under three verses only where it is a complete word-event unit, and this span is not one, since its onset is a dateline rather than a word-event; the guard then names this very span as held, so the exception is recorded but its ground is not.
- The book's planning text states the size of the messenger chain at MT 36:2-36:7 twice and the two statements disagree, once as six and once as seven. The witness bytes read six occurrences on six verses and the formula census agrees, and the row followed the six.
- Whether the seam at MT 36:23/36:24 should exist at all, rather than merely carry a lower confidence. On the ruled reading a mid-unit utterance formula is paragraph-final unless a fresh onset follows, and no onset follows at MT 36:24; the alternative shape is one row over MT 36:16-36:32 with a cap disclosure.
- At MT 35:14 and MT 35:15 the pronominal object addressed to Mount Seir is written in the form that elsewhere marks a feminine singular addressee, while MT 35:3-35:12 addresses it as masculine singular. The ordinary pausal reading is masculine, and this lane treats it as such.
- Whether the material from Ezek.39.1 restates the oracle opened at Ezek.38.1 or advances it cannot be settled from these two witnesses. The two refrains at Ezek.39.6 and Ezek.39.7 bear directly on it and were not in the evidence when the question was held.
- Whether Ezek.39.25-Ezek.39.29 is the closing coda of the Gog material or the restoration collection's own closing salvation unit. Both readings survive these bytes; the label rests on the salvation vocabulary of the span (reversal of captivity, mercy on the whole house of Israel, jealousy for the holy name, the poured-out spirit) rather than on any boundary device.
- The nations-facing unit label on Ezek.39.21-Ezek.39.24 rests on the larger span it sits inside rather than on anything in the four verses: there is no foreign addressee, no set-your-face opener and no verdict against a nation, and the only refrain is in Israel's mouth at Ezek.39.22.
- Paseq is available as a count per verse only, so no position inside any verse of this cluster was checked or asserted; the paragraph marks are a single witness throughout.
- Whether the six gate circuits of Ezek.40.20-Ezek.40.37 are one list that may not be cut inside it, or two groups divided by the court-change transport verb at Ezek.40.28.
- Whether the hundred-cubit notice at Ezek.40.19 closes the court description or the gate circuit measured before it.
- Every unit in this cluster carries one evidence-mirror flag. Answered: in each case the unmirrored verses are the comparison spans and the rejected-alternative ranges cited in the reasoning, plus the adjacent seam verse on one or both sides; none of them is a defect of literary form and none moves a boundary. What remains open is whether the adjacent seam verse must be carried in the structured evidence list at all, since some units here carry it, at Ezek.39.29, Ezek.40.19, Ezek.41.12 and Ezek.41.13, while others argue it in prose only, at Ezek.40.4, Ezek.40.17, Ezek.40.20, Ezek.40.38, Ezek.40.48, Ezek.41.4 and Ezek.41.5.
- The disclosure sentence for the disjunctive-stroke layer is worded near-identically in five of this cluster's eight units.
- Every row in this cluster sits inside the dateline block opened at MT 40:1, and none of the seven names that block; the row schema carries no field for it.
- Where the paragraph mark after MT 43:9 and the two after MT 43:27 fall within their verses cannot be settled from the staged extract, and the same limit applies to the disjunctive stroke counts in chapters 41, 42 and 43.
- Whether the seam inside MT 41:12-41:26 belongs at 41:16 or at 41:15 turns on how the closing clause of 41:15 is weighed, since it names the structures the following description then takes up.
- Whether any ketiv/qere outside the chapter-40 cluster other than MT 42:16 falls on a numeral was not swept across the book by this lane.
- Whether the argued-but-unmirrored pattern should be read as a per-row defect or as a corpus-level convention. It stands on 6 of this cluster's 8 rows and on 115 of the 145 rows corpus-wide, so it is systemic rather than local. This review therefore charged it only where the unmirrored verse carries a whole seam warrant, at the onset at 45.1 and at both seams of the statute at 46.16-46.18, and named it without charging it where the seam has its own cited device, at the onsets at 44.1, 45.9, 45.18 and 46.1.
- Whether the 14-16 verse allowance extends by its reasoning to statute chapters, or is closed to the measured and listed blocks it names. Two rows here run past the 7-10 target (17 verses at 44.15-44.31 and 15 at 46.1-46.15) and both cite that allowance; on the closed reading neither may, though each is genuinely uncuttable for want of an internal onset device.
- Whether 44.4 belongs with the vision scene above it or heads the statute below it. This review reads it with the scene, on the movement verb and the prostration, and the row is supported on that reading; the alternative division is disclosed by the row and is not unreasonable.
- Intra-verse positions are unavailable from this witness's extract for the parashah marks and the paseq occurrence this cluster relies on, so every mark claim here is checked only at verse granularity and remains single-witness.
- Two checks in the standing checklist do not bind this cluster and were therefore not run as boundary work: the prose puncta hand-check, whose two sites are MT 41.20 and MT 46.22 and both fall outside this span, and the numbering-zone dual-reference rule, which touches no verse in chs 44-46. No row here makes a puncta claim, which is the correct state.
- Paseq disclosure is asymmetric across this cluster and I cannot resolve whether the omission is a defect. The paseq inventory lists occurrences inside two rows that disclose none - MT 47:9 and MT 47:12 twice in the row spanning 47:1-47:12, and MT 47:16 and MT 47:17 in the row spanning 47:13-47:23 - while the four rows in ch 48 disclose the occurrences in their spans (MT 48:1, 48:7, 48:22, 48:35).
- The transport-verb sweep's membership at MT 46:21. The sweep of 20 verses does not include 46:21, yet 46:21 opens with a transport verb of the same family and carries a second one, as the row itself discloses; measured over this cluster's span the transport forms fall at MT 46:19, 46:21, 47:1, 47:2 and 47:6, which is one verse more than the sweep names here.
- Whether the low-severity findings on the two mark-absence rows should instead be recorded as passes. Both absence statements are measured true, and my challenge is against their form - a denial carried inside a comma chain, and an impossibility asserted where only an absence is measurable.
- Whether the rule against a denial placed behind a comma reaches past dateline claims. The same shape stands on device-classification statements in three further rows of this cluster, where a mark is called texture and then, after a comma, not a cut.
- Two rows attach a caveat about position inside a verse to a parashah mark, at MT 1:28 and at MT 3:16, and one of them declines on that basis to place the mark relative to the charge in MT 3:17.
- The unmirrored-reference flag on the row covering MT 3:22-3:27 lists MT 4:2 through MT 4:17, none of which that row argues; only MT 4:1 and MT 4:8 are argued there.
- MT 2:6 carries the full rebellious-house refrain verse-finally and is not named in the row covering MT 2:1-2:7, whose close rests on the shorter form at MT 2:7.
- No row in this cluster carries a dated-block disclosure, and the row shape itself has no field for one.
- Whether a closed-section mark standing exactly on a seam a row explicitly rejects raises the duty above disclosure-as-corroboration — the case at MT 4:3, where the mark falls on the rejected division and that verse also ends on a sign declaration.
- The inventory does not record where inside a verse a section mark falls, so I could not test whether any mark in this span sits inside its verse rather than after it.
- Whether the two short units at 7:5-7:9 and 7:10-7:27 are the right shape for the 'end' oracle at all: the Masoretic sectioning of this chapter runs 7:1-4, 7:5-22, 7:23-27, which agrees with the row division at 7:4/7:5 and with nothing else in it.
- The witness is a single manuscript tradition. Every paragraph mark, disjunctive stroke, ketiv/qere site and editorial note cited here rests on that one witness, and setumah is not treated as equal to petuchah.
- The printed device form for the vision-transport frame carries a preposition that MT 1:1 does not — the same shape as the already-retained low where a hand idiom is printed in a form one of its seven verses does not carry. This second instance is not recorded in anything I read.
- The retained-lows record binds the toolkit at a digest that no longer matches the file, and the current toolkit already prints the one-clause cure that record offered.
- Whether the wheel description stands as its own unit or as texture inside the material on either side is left open by the book's ruled strategy, and with its lexical ground withdrawn the span rests on the narration-versus-description alternation alone.
- Whether the internal paragraph division after MT 11:1 should be read as arguing a competing seam at MT 11:2, splitting the transport verse from the address about the city's schemers.
- Whether printing a read form for a ketiv/qere site under an original-language reference is an accepted corpus class rather than a defect.
- Straight versus curly apostrophe inside translation quotations is inconsistent across the corpus, not only in my cluster.
- Three of my seven rows carry the seven-word run about a span holding no ketiv/qere site and no paseq, which the current reuse report places at eight rows against a ten-row hard gate.
- Whether the utterance signature at MT 14:16, MT 14:18 and MT 14:20 is grammatically attached to the oath or merely co-occurs in the verse cannot be settled from the witness, which does not carry placement within a verse. A re-derivation of the oath sweep returns 16 verses over the book, of which 14 also carry the long signature somewhere in the verse, against a ruled figure of 13 carrying it attached.
- Whether a three-verse row opened by a messenger formula rather than by a word-event satisfies the over-split floor. The floor's named three-verse precedent is a complete word-event unit, and a cut at MT 14:21 would leave a three-verse messenger-opened unit.
- The retained-lows record binds the toolkit file at a digest that no longer matches it. The file at its current digest already carries both cures that record offered, so the drift looks like the intended folding of those cures into a later edit rather than an unreviewed change.
- The editorial remark at MT 14:14 and MT 14:20 is recorded at verse level only; its attachment to the variant word is an inference, although the variant site is the only one in each of those two verses.
- Two of the six rows carry, in their span-level denial of a variant site and a paseq, one of the seven-word runs already standing at eight rows; a third row adopting that wording would break the reuse gate.
- Whether the petuchah recorded on MT 16:35 follows that verse or falls inside it cannot be settled from the staged extract, only from the underlying markup.
- Three rows of this cluster place the strict word-event onset among their observed signals at first verses that carry no such onset, namely MT 16:15, MT 16:20 and MT 16:24; one of the three anchors the shared frame to MT 16:1 by verse and two do not.
- The two rows opening at MT 16:35 and MT 16:44 disclose no shared word-event frame at all, although the next strict onset after MT 16:1 stands at MT 17:1, so those spans also sit inside the MT 16:1 unit.
- Several references argued in prose are not carried in the rows' structured evidence references, chiefly MT 16:1 on the row over MT 16:15-16:19, MT 16:36 on the row over MT 16:24-16:34, and MT 16:51 and MT 16:58 on the row over MT 16:44-16:50.
- The corpus second-person-singular recognition category carries a masculine label and a masculine citation form, while the form at MT 16:62 is second-person feminine singular, as is the form at the one other Israel-addressed member of that category; the remaining three members are masculine.
- The corpus ruled reading records a poetry-marked block beginning at MT 18:5, but the pinned mark extract carries only the paragraph marks, the paseq, the maqqef and the verse-end stroke, and records no stichography segment anywhere in chapter 18.
- Three rows in this cluster place the count-only limitation on a positive paseq claim behind a comma rather than stating it as its own fact, and the standalone sentence form of that same limitation is at the reuse ceiling.
- The mirroring check on argued references flags six of this cluster seven rows, and the one row it does not flag is the row whose load-bearing promise carries no verse reference at all.
- Two read forms in this row set are printed with the source morpheme separator removed, and the corpus normalizer dry run reports zero defects while bucketing sixty-three read forms as their own class.
- At MT 18:20 the read form of the ketiv/qere is given in the row with the source's morpheme separator removed, and in that form it is not an exact substring anywhere in the witness; the separated form is what the variant inventory carries, and the running text carries only the unpointed written form. Recorded here as a site, not adjudicated, and the non-witness string is not reproduced.
- The row covering MT 19 carries a parable-class figure-shift signal for a chapter whose only genre label is the lament noun, at MT 19:1 and twice at MT 19:14. The parable noun does not occur in MT 19; the two parable-root forms in the chapter, at MT 19:11 and MT 19:14, are the ruling sense rather than the genre word. Whether that signal class is licensed for a lament-labelled unit is a taxonomy question rather than a byte question, so it is recorded rather than filed as a defect.
- The cut between MT 18:9 and MT 18:10 falls inside a single word-event unit and rests on a refrain close alone - the verse-final utterance formula at MT 18:9 - with no adjacent parashah mark and with no fresh therefore at the onset, which is the pairing named where an internal cut rests on a refrain close.
- Inside MT 20:1-26 the two sabbath notices are described together as gifts, but on the bytes MT 20:12 states the giving while MT 20:20 is a command to hallow what was given.
- Whether a row whose span carries the single-witness editorial-note layer must disclose it. Across this book, 27 of the 37 spans that carry the layer mention it and 10 do not, and the two twin units in the numbering zone split, the earlier disclosing it and the later not.
- The 21-verse second-person-plural you-shall-know set that two of these rows rely on is carried by the ruled book strategy and not by the frame inventory, whose own second-person entry is a different, five-verse singular set.
- No ketiv/qere site falls anywhere in MT 20:1-21:10, so the already-routed question about read forms rendered without the source's morpheme separator, or read forms occurring only in the apparatus, has zero instances in my cluster.
- The routed read-form question produced no instance in this cluster, and the reason is worth recording as data. The witness extract carries no morpheme separator character anywhere in the book; the only ketiv/qere verse in MT ch 21 is MT 21:28 = WEB 21:23; its read form occurs nowhere in the running text, so it is apparatus-only; and the row covering that verse names the variant site in words and quotes only the written form as the running text prints it.
- Two rows disclose a paseq occurrence and then place the sourcing limitation after a comma - for MT 21:20 = WEB 21:15, and for MT 21:24 and MT 21:27 (= WEB 21:19 and WEB 21:22). This lane does not read that as a defect, because the count-only limitation is exactly the disclosure the mark record itself requires and the position genuinely cannot be recovered from this extract; but whether the comma-placed-denial rule reaches a sourcing limitation, as distinct from a dateline or a puncta denial, is a form question this lane cannot settle.
- Three rows argue the parashah mark behind their onset in prose without listing it in the structured evidence list, while listing every mark that falls inside their own span. All of those mark claims are true against the record in both directions, so this lane treats the split as a consistent corpus practice rather than a defect, but whether the both-sides citation requirement extends to the mark behind the onset is not this lane's to decide.
- If the cut at MT 21:19 = WEB 21:14 is taken, the unit type, confidence and parent collection of the resulting MT 21:19-22 = WEB 21:14-17 unit are not set here, and neither is the confidence of the remaining MT 21:13-18 = WEB 21:8-13 unit.
- Routed open question, instances collected not adjudicated: in all six textual-variant sites inside MT 22:1-24:27 the read form recorded in the mark file occurs nowhere in the running text of its own verse, because the running text carries the unpointed written form instead. The sites are MT 22:18, 23:14, 23:16, 23:42, 23:43 (two notes on the one verse) and 24:2. Two of the six, MT 22:18 and MT 23:16, additionally carry the source's morpheme separator inside both halves of the recorded field. No row in cluster c11 renders any read form: each of the five rows whose span covers such a site names it and withholds the read form in words, so the class produces no instance at row level in this cluster.
- The mark-symmetry check reaches the mark standing immediately behind a row's onset but not marks inside a row's own span. Two of the gaps I report, the setumah at MT 23:35 inside Ezekiel 23:22-35 and the setumah at MT 24:24 inside Ezekiel 24:15-27, are invisible to it for that reason.
- Whether the parent collection whose hard seams Ezekiel 23:36-49 lists is meant as the dated collection running from MT chapter 20, in which case the list is incomplete as I report, or as its MT chapters 22 to 24 portion only, in which case the list is exact and only its scope wording is loose. I judged it on the collection name the row itself carries.
- Read-form rendering sites, recorded and not adjudicated, at Ezek.25.7 and Ezek.25.9. At Ezek.25.7 the written form stands unpointed in the running text and collates there, while the read form as the row renders it is absent from Ezek.25.7 and occurs in the running text only at Ezek.36.4. At Ezek.25.9 the written form likewise collates in the running text, while the read form as rendered occurs nowhere in the book's running text. The witness's variant record carries the written and read forms as one string bearing the source's morpheme separators, and both rows state in-field that they removed those separators.
- The first-foreign-addressee wording challenged on Ezekiel 25:1-7 is not the row's alone: the ruled book strategy states the same thing for the collection onset at Ezek.25.2, while MT 21:33 (= WEB 21:28) addresses the children of Ammon earlier in the book.
- Two pinned inputs disagree on the any-form word-event count: the ruled strategy's never-blend sentence names 49 for the any-form, while the device inventory records 41 for the any-form and 48 for the distinct family total. The rows in this cluster cite 41 and match the inventory.
- Disclosure locality for variant sites: the suite warns where a Hebrew quotation binds to a ketiv/qere verse without the variant disclosed in the same field, and Ezekiel 25:1-7 draws that warn for its closing-refrain citation at Ezek.25.7 even though the row discloses the variant three times elsewhere in itself. Nine rows corpus-wide draw the same warn.
- The refs-mirror flag on all six rows resolves to two causes and to no false Hebrew fact: verses swept out of the verse ranges named in the rejected alternatives, and neighbour-seam verses argued in prose without being mirrored as evidence. Mirroring practice is uneven across the six - the behind-neighbour is mirrored at Ezek.24.27 and Ezek.25.17 and the forward neighbour at Ezek.26.1, but the forward neighbour at Ezek.25.8 is argued and not mirrored.
- Read-form rendering, instances only, not adjudicated. Four ketiv/qere sites fall in this cluster: MT 27:3, MT 27:6, MT 27:15 and MT 28:3. The running Masoretic text carries only the written form at each - הישבתי at MT 27:3, כתים at MT 27:6, והובנים at MT 27:15 and מדנאל at MT 28:3 - and the read form occurs nowhere in that running text. At MT 27:3, MT 27:15 and MT 28:3 the mark record holds the read form with the source's morpheme separator and the rows render it with the separator removed; at MT 27:6 the record holds no separator and the row's rendering agrees with the record. The rows disclose the removal in words. The non-witness strings are deliberately not reproduced here.
- Scope of the comma-placed-denial rule. It was applied here to denials about puncta and about datelines, and this cluster carries neither. One denial of a different kind does sit behind a comma, in the evidence label for MT 26:19, which denies an addressee-class shift in that position.
- The size question on the whole-chapter row covering MT 27. The Masoretic layer gives no internal seam at all, so this lens can only report that nothing in the mark or formula record argues for a cut; whether the span is nonetheless too long is a question for the form lens and the size guidance, not for the bytes.
- Which joint the corpus should treat as the seam between the first two units of MT ch 29: the tradition marks MT 29:7/29:8 with a setumah, while a refrain inside MT 29:9 plus a fresh therefore-verdict at MT 29:10 supports MT 29:9/29:10.
- One row in this cluster states the absence of variant sites and paseq in its span using a seven-word run that already stands on eight rows of the corpus.
- Whether the emphatic-close-plus-mark-plus-messenger-formula pattern should also license a cut at MT 30:9/30:10, where the refrain stands one verse before the mark rather than in the verse the mark follows.
- In Ezekiel 30:20-26 the Masoretic division inside the span, the setumah after Ezek.30.21, is disclosed but not weighed: the rejected alternative argues only the close-side choice between Ezek.30.25 and Ezek.30.26. On the evidence the mark does not cut, because the messenger formula at Ezek.30.22 keeps the same addressee and no refrain closes before it, so I record this as a completeness question about the argument and not as a defect in a fact or a boundary.
- In Ezekiel 32:17-32 the genre call is written with its denial set off by commas, held and then not resolved. Whether that construction falls under the rule that a denial placed behind a comma is a rewording order is a form question above my lens. The device denials in my cluster are not of that shape: each stands clause-initial after a semicolon with its negator directly before the device denied, and each is true against the recorded inventory.
- Nothing in the automatic layer compares a dated onset's asserted month against the date terms its verse actually carries; the arm that fails a date claim for a missing year word is scoped to the month-and-day festival sites of the temple-law chapters. So the month asserted at Ezek.32.17 passes every machine member, and the report I was given reads green on all four of my rows.
- Routed open question, instances collected and not adjudicated: this cluster contains zero read-form renderings. All four rows name their ketiv/qere sites, at Ezek.31.5, Ezek.32.31 and Ezek.32.32, without rendering a read form at all, and one row states explicitly that the note at Ezek.32.32 attaches to the written form and not to the utterance signature it quotes from that verse. The only unpointed written form standing inside a quotation is the one at Ezek.32.32, and it is there because that quotation is the whole verse exactly as the witness carries it.
- Whether MT 33:20 is the one verse the whole-book sof-pasuq total implies lacks that glyph.
- Whether a coordinate chain of denials counts as a denial placed behind a comma for the rewording rule.
- No instance of the routed read-form question arose in this cluster.
- Instances for the already-routed read-form question, recorded and not adjudicated. Seven read forms are rendered across this cluster: one at MT 35:9, one at MT 35:12, two at MT 36:13, two at MT 36:14 and one at MT 36:15. None of the seven occurs anywhere in the running-text extract, which carries the written forms at those sites; each reconstructs its witness note entry exactly, codepoint for codepoint, once the morpheme separator is removed; and three of the seven entries carry a separator in the witness while four carry none. I reproduce none of the seven here and name the sites instead.
- The per-cluster flag listing I was handed is incomplete for this cluster. It lists one reference-mirror flag on each of five rows. The digest-bound suite also carries, standing on these same six rows, one translation-quotation flag and nineteen universal-quantifier flags; those members identify their flags by a key the listing's builder did not read, so they were not attributed to any cluster.
- Whether a row must disclose a non-variant editorial note carried by a verse inside its span. The witness carries such a note on MT 36:7, recording an accent read differently from the printed edition, and the row covering MT 36:1-36:12 cites MT 36:7 as evidence without disclosing the note. The note layer is described as single-witness apparatus to be disclosed, but the corpus is not uniform: of the thirty-six rows whose spans cover such a note, twenty-six disclose it and ten do not. I did not score this against the row, because the expectation is not settled and the device the row cites at that verse does not turn on accents.
- The reference-mirror flags on five of my rows are answered as legitimate, with one exception I did not score. On four of them the unmirrored references are produced by prose that names a larger span or a comparison site, each labelled as comparison or as a shared frame, and none is offered as that row's own boundary evidence. The exception is the row covering MT 36:24-36:32, whose boundary evidence list includes MT 11:19: that reference is a lexical contrast twenty-five chapters away and bears on neither of the row's two seams, and the flag did not catch it.
- Instances of the already-routed read-form question, collected and not adjudicated. Both of its sub-classes stand in this cluster and both stand in row Ezekiel 37:15-28. At MT 37:16 and MT 37:19 the row's read form is printed without the morpheme separator the source notation carries, so the string it prints occurs nowhere in the witness - neither in the running text nor in the note. At MT 37:22, in the same row, the read form is an exact copy of the source notation, and at MT 39:25 in row Ezekiel 39:25-29 it is likewise an exact copy; in both of those cases the form occurs nowhere in the running text of its own verse, because the running text there carries the written form. Four sites in total, in two rows.
- The inventory's residue list of year words that are not datelines is derived from a singular-form year sweep, and so does not name MT 39:9, which carries a plural year word in a stated duration - the seven years the weapons are burned - inside the span of row Ezekiel 39:1-10.
- MT 39:28 carries two sanctioned sweep labels at once: the device inventory places it in the strict 28-verse recognition list, while the ruled strategy names it among the expansions belonging to the wide 64-verse family.
- Row Ezekiel 39:21-24 prints a translation gloss of MT 39:29 with a straight apostrophe where the translation prints a typographic one, and carries no in-field translation reference for that gloss.
- The guidance that holds a numbered or refrain list together names the gate circuits at MT 40:20-37 as a list not cut inside, while the guidance that pre-rules where the measurement rows cut names exactly the split these two rows carry, at MT 40:27/40:28.
- Several English renderings in this cluster reproduce the public translation's wording of the same verse run for run, while being presented as renderings of the Hebrew rather than as translation quotations: the strongest sites are MT 40:28 (eleven words), MT 40:4 (ten words) and MT 40:48 (nine words).
- The guided-motion verb at MT 40:24 is treated by one row as a transport verb held as intra-row texture, but the ruled twenty-verse class does not list that verse.
- ROUTED OPEN QUESTION, INSTANCES COLLECTED AND NOT ADJUDICATED. Every written-form quotation in this cluster is rendered with the source's morpheme separator stripped: the corpus variant record carries those separators and the rows do not. The five sites are the galleries form at MT 41:15, the paired chamber and entrance forms at MT 42:9, the cubit-word at MT 42:16, the form-word at MT 43:11 and the altar-hearth word at MT 43:16. Each stripped rendering is nevertheless an exact substring of the running witness text at its own verse, because the running text itself prints the written form unpointed and unseparated. I found no read-form quotation anywhere in this cluster, so I have no apparatus-only read-form instance to add.
- The untyped note at MT 43:15 says the edition has abandoned or added a ketiv/qere there relative to the printed critical edition. The corpus record of variant sites has no entry at that verse, which fits the abandoned branch, but the note text is disjunctive and I cannot settle which branch applies from the extract alone.
- The do-not-reuse census I measured over the corpus rows returns 8 rows for seven of the eight named runs, matching what my brief states, but 7 rows and not 8 for the run about a strict word-event onset.
- Paseq positions inside a verse cannot be placed from the extract, so for all seven rows I verified paseq occurrence counts and verse identities only, never an intra-verse position.
- One row in this cluster carries its substrate-signal field as a bare string where the other six carry a list, at MT 43:13-43:17.
- Whether the corpus sanctions writing a read form with the source's morpheme separator removed, and whether a read form occurring nowhere in the running text may be presented as a quotation. Three instances stand in this cluster: one read form at Ezek.44.5 and two at Ezek.44.24. Each is recorded here as a site with its verse; none is adjudicated and none of the three non-witness strings is reproduced.
- Whether a row must disclose a variant site its span merely covers, or only one a quotation binds to. Two rows here omit disclosure: Ezek.45.3 and Ezek.45.5 in one span, Ezek.46.9 and Ezek.46.15 in the other. The variant layer's own note states span coverage as the trigger and 54 of the 59 rows in the book that cover such a site disclose it, so the corpus norm is clear; but this lens's instruction names quotation binding.
- The severity band for a comma-placed dateline denial. The governing rule calls such a denial a rewording order and never a pass, which reads as a hard form defect, but the fact denied is true in both instances found here, so both are recorded at medium as form defects rather than as false claims.
- Three short English phrases in this cluster, written in straight single quotes as glosses, are also verbatim translation wording at their cited verse. The corpus's own quote check looks for curly single quotes, so straight-quoted verbatim wording is outside its coverage. None is charged against a row here, because the corpus convention marks a translation quotation by a double-curly delimiter plus an in-field translation reference and treats a straight-quoted phrase as a gloss, and each of the three is short enough to be the natural rendering of the Hebrew it glosses.
- Chapter-level length allowances. The ruled strategy grants the longer per-row verse allowance to the measured and listed blocks of chapters 40 to 42, 45 and 48. Two rows here lie in chapters 44 and 46 and invoke that allowance - one to say it exceeds it, which is harmless, and one to claim cover under it, which this lens flags but does not own.
- The permanent prose-puncta hand-check and the comma-separated puncta denial hand-check do not fire on this cluster, and this is recorded so their absence is not mistaken for an omission: the two puncta verses those checks name lie outside Ezek.44.1-Ezek.46.18, the nearer of them falling in the row immediately after this cluster's last.
- Five read-forms in these rows are rendered with the source's morpheme separator stripped and are not substrings of the running text: the read form after the northward-facing rooms at MT 46:19, and the read forms at MT 47:8, MT 47:10, MT 47:11 and MT 47:12. The witness's apparatus carries each with its separator, and in three of the five the written form is separated as well. Recorded as sites; the non-witness strings are deliberately not reproduced here.
- Paseq occurrences are under-enumerated across this whole cluster, not in one row only: one within each of MT 48:2 through MT 48:6 unnamed by the northern-portions row, one within MT 48:18 and three within MT 48:21 unnamed by the holy-portion row, one each within MT 48:24, MT 48:25 and MT 48:26 unnamed by the southern-portions row, and one each within MT 47:16 and MT 47:17 unnamed by the border row.
- Two of the seven rows carry a different writer attempt identifier from the other five, inside the same part.
- The ground that a 29-verse undivided row would exceed every cap, used to reject the single-row alternative for this chapter, is not supported by the ruled cap posture, which admits longer whole-chapter spans at reduced confidence with a cap disclosure.
- Whether an undisclosed in-span messenger formula, son-of-man address or recognition member is a DEFECT. Both c02 lanes assert a duty to 'disclose the frame devices its span carries by role', and both filed findings on it at MT 6:2, 6:3, 6:13 and 7:2. None of the 29 rulings handed to me establishes that duty. a standing review requirement rules only that a key naming a device the span does not contain is a defect and that a missing kq.* key is not, which implies but does not state that other missing keys are defects.
- Whether a standing review requirement's five-word bright line should carry a formula carve-out. MEASURED: of the 34 qualifying run-and-verse pairs in my group, 8 are at exactly five words and several of those are the recognition formula itself ('know that I am Yahweh' at 6:7, 6:10, 6:14) or a generic phrase ('to the house of Israel' at 3:4), where a row rendering the Hebrew independently will coincide with the WEB by necessity rather than by quotation.
- The 7:9/7:10 seam, and with it whether ch 7 tiles as two rows or three. MEASURED: the marks put the tradition's sections at 7:1-4, 7:5-22, 7:23-27; the corpus divides 7:1-4, 7:5-9, 7:10-27; no mark stands at 7:9 and MT 7:10 is in no pinned onset class.
- Whether the two 'none coincides with a formula onset' occurrences in Ezekiel 5:1-17's device_notes should be scored as one defect or two. I answered both as genuine so that the count of genuine flags is not understated by a tokenisation choice, but they are one sentence and one error.
- The peer_round_scoping.v2.json builder's own recorded near/far discrepancy with the ruling (819-against-708 far verses on an agreed denominator of 948) is untouched by anything I did.
- A ruling I believe is under-specified rather than wrong, disclosed as the brief directs rather than departed from: a standing review requirement's third clause says a mark on the LAST verse 'stands at the close seam', and the requirement to disclose a section mark in each direction it bears on says 'inside the span' means first verse through last-but-one. Together they are consistent. But a standing review requirement's second clause - a mark on the row's FIRST verse 'stands inside the row (after that verse)' - makes the 3:16 PE in Ezekiel 3:16-21 simultaneously an interior mark and the mark standing at the 3:16/3:17 seam, which is the row's first internal junction.
- PASEQ IS UNAVAILABLE TO ME AND I DID NOT LET TWO CORRELATED LANES SUBSTITUTE FOR IT. Ezek_oshb.txt contains zero U+05C0 characters (measured), and no disjunctive-stroke inventory is among the exact paths my launch message or brief gives me. Every paseq claim in every row and in both packets is therefore REPORTED. This touches Ezekiel 8:1-6 (three verses), Ezekiel 8:7-13, Ezekiel 8:14-18 (the unsupported back-reference), Ezekiel 9:1-11 (four occurrences), Ezekiel 10:1-8 and Ezekiel 12:21-25. - KETIV/QERE AND THE OSHB EDITORIAL APPARATUS ARE UNAVAILABLE TO ME. No note-layer file is among my exact paths and the staged witness carries no morpheme slash (measured: zero '/' characters in Ezek_oshb.txt). Every K/Q and editorial-note claim on Ezekiel 8:1-6, Ezekiel 9:1-11, Ezekiel 11:14-21, Ezekiel 12:8-16 and Ezekiel 12:17-20 is REPORTED. Where I could measure a proxy I did: the defective spelling דנאל at exactly three verses is what carries my answer on OL's Qere-adaptation finding, and the absence of עזרו from the whole witness is what makes OL's read-form fact true even though a standing review requirement bars the challenge.
- a standing review requirement's aggregate discrepancy is unresolved upstream and I did not resolve it. peer_round_scoping.v2.json records the ruling measuring 708 of 948 flagged verses FAR against the builder's 819, a 111-verse gap the builder routes to the controlling agent. I applied the RULE per verse as the brief and the file both instruct, and on my 12 flags the rule and the split agreed everywhere, so nothing in my answers depends on the aggregate. I report the agreement as evidence bearing on the gap, not as a resolution of it.
- a standing review requirement's REACH IS GENUINELY UNCERTAIN AND I FLAG IT RATHER THAN DECIDING IT. a standing review requirement says the quotation duty attaches 'regardless of delimiter' to five-or-more-word runs identical to the WEB. Applied literally that reaches unquoted descriptive English that coincides with WEB wording ('the man clothed in linen', 'the threshold of the house'), which would make almost any English rendering of a Hebrew phrase a quotation. I filed only the four instances that are presented AS quotations and left the rest as a question for the controlling agent. My scan was also first-hit-per-verse and under-reports; it is indicative, not a census.
- a standing review requirement's SILENCE ON MISSING DEVICE KEYS IS AN INFERENCE, NOT A MEASUREMENT. a standing review requirement condemns a key naming an absent device and exempts only 'a missing kq.* key'. My five C-4 disclosure findings rest on reading that single exemption as exhaustive. The devices are measurably in-span; the DUTY is inferred, and I say so at every instance.
- WHETHER AN ABSENT ONSET-SEAM MARK OWES AN AFFIRMATIVE STATEMENT IS NOT SETTLED BY THE RULINGS. the requirement to disclose a section mark in each direction it bears on speaks of disclosing 'the mark'; the toolkit says absence must be proven, not assumed. Ezekiel 9:1-11 and Ezekiel 11:1-13 never state that no mark stands at their onset seam (and Ezekiel 11:1-13 asserts one that is interior). I named this at LOW on Ezekiel 9:1-11 and folded it into the a standing review requirement finding on Ezekiel 11:1-13, and I did not invent a duty.
- MY CONFIDENCE PROPOSALS ARE JUDGEMENTS, NOT MEASUREMENTS, AND I LABELLED THEM SO. The Ezekiel 11:14-21 reduction is conditional on how the author wave weighs the 11:16/11:17 interior candidate, and my refusal of the Ezekiel 10:9-17 increase rests on reading a standing review requirement and the brief's counts rule together - a reading, not a byte.
- I DID NOT RE-DERIVE THE INVENTORY'S OWN LISTS EXHAUSTIVELY. I reproduced the recognition classes (28 / 5 / and the unlisted 21) and spot-measured membership for every class I cited on my rows, but I did not re-measure all 122 messenger-formula or 93 son-of-man verses. Those memberships are MEASURED as to the verses on my rows and taken as pinned-inventory otherwise.
- CORRELATION IS MY STANDING LIMIT, NOT A CAVEAT. I am an Anthropic-family model reconciling two Anthropic-family lanes. Where I agree with a lane I have tried to say what I measured myself rather than that the lane persuaded me, and on the three both-support rows I checked the agreement against the witness anyway and found a defect on each. Where I could not measure - paseq, the note layer - I have refused to let their agreement stand in for the measurement, and that refusal is recorded above rather than resolved.
- Whether Ezekiel 14:1-11's confidence should fall to medium or further. What I measured: with a standing review requirement applied, the onset at Ezek.14.1 has no counted device and the sectioning, read in its recorded direction, is adverse to it, while the close side is fully corroborated. Medium is my reading of that balance.
- The 16:22/16:23 against 16:23/16:24 seam on Ezekiel 16:20-23. - Whether the ABSENCE of a mark at the close or onset seam is owed as an affirmative disclosure. the requirement to disclose a section mark in each direction it bears on says every row owes disclosure of the mark in three directions and that UNAVAILABLE is a value and never a blank, but where no mark exists there is no mark to name. - The §7 confidence band. §7 line 361 directs medium_low/low for the regions it names and three of my rows inside those named questions carry high. I propose the move on one row only.

- I did not verify the ruling artifact itself. ezek_controlling_agent_ruling_e12.v1.json is listed in the brief's table but the 29 wordings were read from the brief, and my blindness constraint forbids reading other rulings, fix-up orders, cure claims, S-reviews and other peers' outputs.
- a standing review requirement's five-word threshold applied to natural renderings. The Ezekiel 25:1-7 finding, 'toward the children of Ammon', is exactly at the threshold and is a phrase a translator and an analyst could arrive at independently from the Hebrew. I applied a standing review requirement as the bright line it is written to be rather than soften it, and I record that the controlling agent may reasonably want a de-minimis carve-out for runs at exactly five words that are forced by the source. I did not depart from the ruling; I am naming the pressure on it.
- Whether a standing review requirement's duty reaches verses INSIDE a row's span at all. a standing review requirement's window is written as [first-1, last+1], which contains the whole span, and its last sentence removes only 'the far-side verse' from per-row scope - so on the plain words my nine in-span findings are per-row defects. But every one of the validator's flags is far-side, which is consistent with an alternative reading in which refs_mirror was only ever about seam verses. If the controlling agent means the narrow reading, all nine of my a standing review requirement findings fall and the ten flags remain correctly out of scope either way. - The tension between a standing review requirement and the strategy's section 2. a standing review requirement says device counts are read from the inventory 'never ... from the strategy's prose'. The 21-verse 2mp recognition class exists only in the strategy's prose, which itself discloses that it is not in the inventory and rules how its members may be cited. Applied mechanically, a standing review requirement would condemn two of my rows for a citation that is correct and well-sourced. I retracted my own finding on it and I cannot settle the rule.
- The width of the 'hard seam' class. book_strategy_Ezek.md section 5 defines it as a dateline or vision onset; its own section 9 applies it to plain word-event onsets.
