- ROUTED UPWARD ON THE a standing review requirement PRINCIPLE, NO ROW SCORED - the transport class is internally inconsistent inside ONE pinned input. book_strategy_Ezek.md enumerates the class as exactly 20 verses and that list omits Ezek.40.24, 40:48, 42:15 and 43:1; the same file's granularity section names 'a transport verb (40:17, 40:28, 40:32, 40:35, 40:48; 41:1; 42:1; 42:15)' as the internal cut sites, and its self-check section asserts 'transport 20'. MEASURED: Ezek.40.48 reads ויבאני and Ezek.42.15 reads והוציאני, the same morphology as the enumerated 40:17, 40:28, 41:1 and 42:1, while Ezek.40.24 and Ezek.43.1 both read ויולכני, which the class gloss's 'led me out' arguably covers. Four of my rows rest an onset or an internal-texture disclosure on this class (Ezekiel 40:20-27 at 40:24, Ezekiel 40:48–41:4 at 40:48, Ezekiel 42:15-20 at 42:15, Ezekiel 43:1-9 at 43:1), and BOTH primary lanes asserted membership for 40:48 and 43:1 that the enumeration does not support. This is precisely the trap of scoring a row against one list when the class is declared elsewhere, so I report the discrepancy upward and score NO row on it. It needs a ruling on whether the enumeration or the gloss governs; if the enumeration governs, it is an undercount in the strategy and not a defect in any row.
- ROUTED UPWARD - my own sweep of ויאמר אלי over the consonantal skeleton of Ezek_oshb.txt returns 35 verses where book_strategy_Ezek.md declares 36. Tier: MEASURED for my regex (whole-word adjacency on the consonantal skeleton), REPORTED for the strategy's 36. Two rows and three lane packets cite 'thirty-six'. No seam and no verdict in my clusters turns on 35 versus 36, and I do NOT present 35 as a correction, because the difference could be a boundary or suffix case my regex excludes. It needs one person to reconcile it once.
- ROUTED UPWARD, AND IT MAY BEAR ON THE a standing review requirement AGGREGATE THE SCOPING BUILDER COULD NOT SETTLE - verse_inventory.json declares no witness face, and MEASURED it is WEB-faced rather than MT: it gives chapter 20 as 49 verses and chapter 21 as 32, whereas web_mt_offset_map.json states that MT chapter 20 has 44 and MT chapter 21 has 37. The scoping file states that its a standing review requirement window split 'resolved adjacent verses across chapter boundaries from verse_inventory.json', and its own unresolved-discrepancy block names 'the MT/WEB numbering zone' as a possible cause of its 111-verse gap against the controlling agent's measurement. A WEB-faced adjacency table applied to MT-faced spans resolves the wrong neighbour for any row touching the MT 20/21 chapter edge. My own rows are in chapters 40-43, where the two faces are identical and both give 39:29, 40:49, 41:26, 42:20 and 43:27, so MY adjacency is unaffected - I checked rather than assumed. I name the mechanism as a candidate cause for other rows and for the the list of repairs aggregate; I read no other peer group's rows and I make no claim about them.
- OPEN CONVENTION QUESTION, FOUR EXACT INSTANCES, AND THE SPLIT AND THE RULE DISAGREE - Ezek.40.28 (Ezekiel 40:20-27), Ezek.40.38 (Ezekiel 40:28-37), Ezek.42.1 (Ezekiel 41:12-26) and Ezek.43.18 (Ezekiel 43:13-17) are each named inside the annotation of ANOTHER entry in the row's boundary_evidence_refs rather than as their own list entry. The scoping split counts all four as in-window defects. Applying the a standing review requirement RULE literally - 'a verse inside the window that the prose argues and the REFERENCE LIST OMITS' - the list does not omit them, so I score them as NOT defects and say so plainly. If the convention is that a structured reference must be its own entry, all four flip to genuine. LF/c19 routed the same carriage question upward from its own cluster. It is a corpus-level answer and I do not decide it.
- OPEN RULING GAP, APPLIED CONSERVATIVELY - a standing review requirement triggers on five or more words identical to the WEB 'at the cited verse'. MEASURED: Ezekiel 42:1-14's strongest_rejected_alternative carries eleven consecutive words identical to WEB Ezek.42.11 ('like the appearance of the rooms which were toward the north') in straight quotes with NO verse cited in that field at all, so the literal trigger is not met although the substance - an unattributed eleven-word translation quotation - is exactly what a standing review requirement addresses. I report it as a genuine quotation-attribution defect on the substance and disclose that it falls outside a standing review requirement's literal wording. Four shorter runs in Ezekiel 40:48–41:4, Ezekiel 41:12-26, Ezekiel 42:1-14 and Ezekiel 42:15-20 match a WEB verse the field does not cite and are common English collocations; I treat those as incidental and do not score them.
- OPEN, ROUTED RATHER THAN DECIDED - the a standing review requirement defect rate on my rows is 33 runs distributed across all fifteen rows, so the exposure is corpus-wide in these clusters rather than a property of any one row, and OL/c19 already recorded three of the strongest instances as undecided data. The tension is real and I disclose it: a literal rendering of plain architectural narrative will often coincide with the WEB run for run, so some of the 33 may be convergence rather than quotation. But a standing review requirement as written sets the bar at five consecutive words 'regardless of delimiter' and admits no coincidence exception, and I applied the ruling rather than departing from it. If the ruling is meant to exempt convergent literal renderings, it should say so, because on my measurement it currently makes every row in two clusters defective.
- OPEN, SIDE-CONSTRAINED - a standing review requirement settles Ezek.40.27/40.28 and I did not reopen it. The wider conflict in the MT 40:20-37 gate-circuit region - list-integrity guidance that a numbered or refrain list is not cut inside it, against the pre-ruled measurement cut - remains untouched for the other gate seams, and both primary lanes routed it upward independently. MEASURED evidence bearing on it that the ruling did not cite: the seven-step ascent at Ezek.40.22 and 40:26 against the eight-step ascent at 40:31, 40:34 and 40:37 groups the gates by court, while the cross-reference refrain כמדות האלה stands at six verses in chapter 40 including once inside the outer-court row at 40:24 - so the refrain's distribution does NOT divide the gates along the court boundary and the step count does. I offer that as evidence for whoever settles the remaining seams, not as a reopening.
- OPEN, AGAINST MY OWN CONCLUSION - on Ezekiel 41:12-26 I decline LF/c20's proposed cut at Ezek.41.16 and keep the fifteen-verse row, but book_strategy_Ezek.md's own chapter-41 block plan reads 'side chambers 41:5-15, decoration 41:15-26', which contemplates a division at 41:15/41:16 and which neither lane cited. The shipped rows cut at 41:11/41:12 instead, so BOTH the shipped seam and LF's proposal depart from the plan. A plan is not a witness and a standing review requirement keeps the decision on the text signals, which is why I keep the row - but I disclose the contrary evidence rather than leaving it for someone else to find.
- UNAVAILABLE, NAMED - intra-verse position for every mark, paseq and puncta in my clusters is UNAVAILABLE from the staged inputs, per a standing review requirement and per pmarks_Ezek.json's own paseq note. I raised no question that turns on one, and I challenged the one row that asserts one (Ezekiel 40:1-4). Fourteen of my fifteen rows state the same 'no intra-verse position is sourceable from the extract' qualifier correctly.
- UNAVAILABLE, NAMED - whether the untyped n="q" note at Ezek.43.15 records an ABANDONED or an ADDED ketiv/qere cannot be settled from the extract, because the note text is disjunctive. It does not change the finding, which is that the variant record carries no site at 43:15 while the row asserts one. OL/c20 reached the same limit independently.
- DISCLOSED CORRELATION LIMIT - on eight of my fifteen rows my conclusion agrees with at least one primary lane. That agreement is three Anthropic-family voices and not confirmation. Every such agreement in this deliverable is carried by a measurement I took myself from the witness bytes or from the pinned inventories, and where I had only the lanes' reasoning and no measurement of my own I said so rather than adding a vote.
- a standing review requirement REPORT UPWARD - transport-class membership of Ezek.44.1. The transport-verb class is not in ezek_device_inventory.json at all; it is declared only in book_strategy_Ezek.md, whose device sweep lists 20 members and names 44:4 while OMITTING 44:1, yet whose temple-vision hard-seam entry calls 44:1 a transport. Two passages of one pinned input disagree about one object.
- a standing review requirement REPORT UPWARD - the law unit's onset verse. The strategy's unit-type list declares temple_law as 44:5-31, while its temple-vision entry describes '44:1-3 the shut gate - own row or the head of 44:4-31' and then '44:4-31 with the Levites/Zadokites contrast'. One pinned input prints 44:5 in one place and 44:4 in another for the same unit's onset.
- SCOPE OF 'ARGUES' IN RULING a standing review requirement. a standing review requirement makes a defect of a verse inside the window that the prose ARGUES and the reference list omits. Applying that to all 15 of my rows, a narrow reading confined to mark-bearing seam verses yields 12 instances, of which the member flagged 11 and missed one (Ezekiel 44:15-31 / Ezek.44.14). A broad reading that counts any in-window verse mentioned near a device or seam word yields 36.
- a standing review requirement SPLIT VERSUS a standing review requirement RULE, resolved to the rule and reported as instructed. peer_round_scoping.v2.json classes Ezek.48.29 as outside the window and not a defect for Ezekiel 48:30-35; the rule places it at first-1, inside. The scoping file itself discloses a larger unresolved discrepancy with the ruling's own near/far measurement, 819-versus-708 far out of an agreed denominator of 948.
- the duty to weigh a competing division reach on an interior mark that is not the row's strongest rejected alternative. Ezekiel 48:8-22 names and weighs the 48:11 samekh in boundary_rationale and device_notes, but not inside strongest_rejected_alternative, whose subject is the undivided 48:1-29 row.
- a standing review requirement's per-verse reach. Three rows disclose no paseq at all where the span carries some, which I filed. Three others disclose some but not all of their paseq verses - Ezekiel 48:1-7 names 2 of 7, Ezekiel 48:8-22 names 1 of 3, Ezekiel 48:23-29 names none of 4.
- OSHB editorial-note layer - UNAVAILABLE to me. the reading from the Hebrew witness asserts a second non-variant editorial note at Ezek.44.3 and another at Ezek.46.12, and the reading from the English and structural apparatus asserts a fidelity note at Ezek.46.12.
- Two lane claims I did not verify. the reading from the Hebrew witness's corpus statistic that 54 of 59 rows covering a variant site disclose it and this is one of five that do not; and the reading from the Hebrew witness's compass assignment of the border sides at 47:15-20.

## 7. Licence and attribution of the witnesses

The Hebrew text is from the Open Scriptures Hebrew Bible, a transcription of the Westminster Leningrad Codex, released into the public domain by its transcribers; the morphological annotation is distributed under the Creative Commons Attribution 4.0 licence and is attributed to the Open Scriptures project. The English text is the World English Bible, which is in the public domain. Quotations of either witness above carry these terms with them. This record makes no claim on either text beyond quotation for the purpose of showing what was argued from it.

## 8. Private terms replaced in quoted prose

The reviewers quoted above wrote for this project, so their prose carried internal codes. Each has been replaced by a phrase naming what it is, mechanically and by whole token. The table is given so a reader can invert any substitution. No reviewer's reasoning has been paraphrased and no sentence was dropped for containing a code.

## 9. The apparatus's own words

Item 20's requirement that nothing be asserted here which is not in a review record is only checkable if the reader can separate the reviewers' words from the frame around them. Every string the generator contributes itself is listed below; everything else in this document is quoted from, or mechanically derived from, a record in the provenance table.

- This document records the division of the book of Ezekiel into units, the disagreements that arose while that division was reviewed, the evidence offered on each side of every disagreement, how each was settled, and what remains unsettled. It is written for a reader who was not present and who owes the process no trust: every claim about the Hebrew text below is quoted or mechanically derived from a review record listed in the provenance table, and the words that are the apparatus's own rather than a reviewer's are enumerated at the end.
- Two witnesses are used throughout. The consonantal and pointed Hebrew is the Open Scriptures Hebrew Bible edition of the Westminster Leningrad Codex; the English is the World English Bible. Neither is emended here. Where the two number verses differently — and in Ezekiel they do — both numbers are given.
- **The two witnesses do not number this book alike.** 
- Every boundary in that stretch therefore has two addresses, and an argument that names only one of them cannot be checked by a reader holding the other witness. This record gives both.
- **The book is long and its repertoire of boundary signals is narrow.** 
- Its unit boundaries are carried by a small set of recurring formulae rather than by varied literary signals, so the same few devices must do almost all the work, and a boundary argument usually turns on which instance of a repeated formula is doing which job.
- Counted in the witness, the recurring devices include 
- **The measuring vision resists ordinary division.** The closing chapters record a guided tour with repeated motion verbs and repeated measurements, in which the natural breaks are architectural rather than rhetorical. Several disagreements below concern that stretch, and they are disagreements about whether a change of place is a change of unit.
- **The confidence recorded on each unit is part of the evidence, not a summary of it.** 
- A unit marked low is not a unit carelessly divided; it is one where the witness itself offers weak or one-sided evidence for the division, and the record says so rather than presenting a confident face.
- The division was reviewed by two independent readings of every unit, neither able to see the other's findings: one working from the Hebrew witness and its accentuation and section marks, one working from the English and the structural apparatus. Their findings were then re-read by a third set of readings covering every unit, and a fourth reading audited those. Each stage was recorded before the next began, and the text under review was fixed by digest throughout, so no stage could quietly revise the object the earlier stage had examined.
- **What this does not establish.** The two initial readings were independent of each other, and that is the strength of the arrangement. The later readings were not independent in the same way: they read the earlier findings. Agreement between them is therefore not evidence that a finding is correct, only that it was not contradicted, and this record never treats such agreement as corroboration. One stage of the review also received descriptions of what earlier readers had found; the adjudicating record held that this biases what a reader looks at rather than what the text says, and declined to discard the round on that ground while recording that its internal agreement cannot be read as independence. That limitation is repeated here because it affects how much weight the reader should give to unanimity below.
- Each entry below names the passage, states the question as a reviewer raised it, gives the evidence offered on each side in that reviewer's own words, and then gives the resolution from whichever record settled it. Where nothing settled it, the entry says so and the matter reappears in section 5.
- Reading from the English and the structural apparatus
- Evidence on each side, as this reader set it out:
- What this reader proposed: 
- Reading from the Hebrew witness
- On the second reading of this passage
- From the witness
- On audit
- Adjudicated
- **Resolution.**
-  The two initial readings reached different conclusions.
- The most consequential fact about this review is what it did not change. 
- The disagreements recorded above are therefore overwhelmingly about the STATED GROUNDS for a boundary rather than about where the boundary falls — about whether the reason given is true to the witness, whether a competing division was weighed, and whether a device the text carries was disclosed. A reader who expected a review of this size to move boundaries should read that as the finding it is.
- Confidence was re-graded on a number of units where the recorded grade overstated or understated the evidence. In every such case the grade moved to match the witness, and no unit's confidence was raised without a reviewer having asked for it.
- The following are recorded as unsettled. They are not oversights; each is a question the evidence available here cannot close.
- Every record this document draws on, with the digest it carried when read. A reader can re-run the generator named below against these files and obtain this document byte for byte.
- Generator: `gen_scholar_record.py`. Checker: `check_scholar_record.py`. Both sit beside this document. The generator is a pure function of the files above: it consults no clock, draws no random values, and iterates every collection in sorted order, so two runs over the same inputs produce the same bytes.
- The Hebrew text is from the Open Scriptures Hebrew Bible, a transcription of the Westminster Leningrad Codex, released into the public domain by its transcribers; the morphological annotation is distributed under the Creative Commons Attribution 4.0 licence and is attributed to the Open Scriptures project. The English text is the World English Bible, which is in the public domain. Quotations of either witness above carry these terms with them. This record makes no claim on either text beyond quotation for the purpose of showing what was argued from it.
- The reviewers quoted above wrote for this project, so their prose carried internal codes. Each has been replaced by a phrase naming what it is, mechanically and by whole token. The table is given so a reader can invert any substitution. No reviewer's reasoning has been paraphrased and no sentence was dropped for containing a code.
